MOVEMENT 16 — THIS STEWARDLY CO-CAPTAIN CO-BOBBINING
“Hailings & Salutationingings!”
Whoa. I nearly dropped the cup.
“Hello?”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining now stands attending TOWARD This One Constitutioning Human.”
I looked at the cup.
“You’re going to need to repeat that.”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining now stands attending TOWARD This One Constitutioning Human.”
I strained to make myself heard in the old telephone box’s mouthpiece THROUGH the Donut Helmet. I was holding the ear cup against the side of the Donut Helmet.
“Got it,” I said. “Some kind of Co-Bobbining. Listen, Co-Bobbining, I’m glad to finally reach customer service around here. Finally, I’m talking to someone who can answer questions about this machine, I presume, because I have more than a few questions.”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining may stand Inquiringmenting AFTER your IN-Questioning-Menting.”
“Uh. Okay. I’m going to take that as a yes.”
I looked out TOWARD the Rail Line.
“First of all, I’m not UN-FOLDing another single thing around here again. At least not until somebody tells me what I’ve been UN-FOLDing myself into around here.
“Because the same thing has been going on ever since I got here. First I had to put together this needle-nozzle thing and drag its string behind me. Then I had to go stick this huge barboning thing here into some kind of clear box. And then I had to impale my Terrestrial Computer with an axle rod THROUGH its square buckle, I might add.
“And then I had to go into this Donut-shaped helmet room and pick up this other Donut-Helmet-shaped thing—you know, this thing that I’m wearing over my head now.”
I pointed at my head.
“And then there’s the Doll.
“What is with the Doll, Co-Bobbining?”
I pointed TOWARD the Doll.
“Look at this thing. This Doll has problems, Co-Bobbining. This Constitutioning Human Doll—so, for one thing, this Constitutioning Human Doll can’t see. For another thing, it doesn’t have any arms. And as far as I can tell, it doesn’t have a nose.
“And it has one weird foot.
“And that foot is in a Unicycle.
“With a single pedal that says RE-STOMP on it.
“And I put together this whole thing myself. And every time I do something around here, it’s just one parts pocket at a time. Do this step. Wait around. Look at the signs. Do this next step. Wait around. Look at the signs.
“Co-Bobbining, I’ve got to tell you, that is my life at every one of these Assemblementing Stations. It’s just another bunch of pieces, but everything somehow just comes to me one thing at a time, and then somehow this machine expects me to be the one who somehow has to put all of these things together around here.
“It’s like IKEA.
“Except there aren’t any pictures on any of the boxes.”
I looked back TOWARD the Rail Line.
“So you can imagine where I’m standing now, Co-Bobbining, I would hope. Because where I’m standing now, I’m standing before a sign on this very Rail Line. I feel like I’ve complied with everything. I’ve done everything that I’ve been told.
“And I checked my work, Co-Bobbining. The Doll wobbles. Not too much, but it wobbles on that Unicycle pedal thing up there.
“But when I UN-FOLDed and saw this whole Dollhouse Assemblementing Station—that’s when I lost it, Co-Bobbining.
“I mean, if you’re going to make me put together an entire Dollhouse now, can you at least show me the picture on the box first?
“Because I’m looking at the same Rail Line that everyone else is looking at, Co-Bobbining. And I’m looking at this sign.
“And I would even just like to know how many pieces. I don’t even need to see the picture. Just tell me how many pieces are in that parts pocket in that Dollhouse Station thing up there.”
“This One Whole Dollhouse Assemblementing FROM HERE stands as One Place.”
“One Place?”
“One Place stands FROM HERE.”
“Yes. That’s not what I asked. I asked how many pieces are in This One Place. How many pieces is this place made out of? You know, how many pieces will I need to stand Assemblementing at each of those Stations inside of that Stationing thing?”
I waited.
Okay.
I waited some more.
I checked to make sure the string hadn’t fallen out of the cup.
It hadn’t.
We still had a connection.
Co-Bobbining just didn’t have anything more to say.
“Well, anyway, while I’ve got you, I do have a handful of comments. And I don’t know whether you’re the one around here who handles these kinds of things, or maybe if you could just pass along some of this input. You know, pass it on to your manager or whoever runs this place.”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining may stand Inquiringmenting AFTER your IN-Questioning-Menting.”
“Okay. Again, I’ll take that as another yes.
“Because I want to be fair, Co-Bobbining. I am not the kind of person who just calls customer service to complain. If something is good, I’m going to tell you it’s good. If something isn’t working, I’m going to tell you it’s not working.
“I don’t keep score.
“I mean, I have a list here, but I’m not keeping score.
“And so far, I hate to tell you this, but this is pretty much a one-star review.
“I thought about it. There have been some adequate things. And possibly a few things that have been slightly delightful since the Weddinging. But there are some real wrinkles here with your deployment. For me, at least.
“And if there’s something you can do about that, or somebody you can talk to, then I think whoever runs this place might like to know what kind of machine we’re all in here.
“And what kind of machine are we all in here?
“Well, first, we’re in the kind of machine where the RE-STEP pedal doesn’t, you know, RE-STEP.”
I pointed down.
I tried it.
Nothing happened.
I tapped it again.
“I’m not sure whether you can hear that THROUGH the telephone-box-airplane thing here, so let me tell you what you would hear if you were here.”
I tapped it again.
“Nothing. See? There’s not even a click. How can somebody out here get this kind of thing fixed?”
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE?”
“Can you hear that?”
I tapped it again.
“I’m tapping RE-STEP over and over again with my foot. I’m practically stomping on the pedal to get to the Wharf FROM HERE. It isn’t doing a thing, which is a problem because This Encounteringmenting Wharf is the whole reason I’m even here. Huckleberry and Enzo are over there waiting on me to figure this out, and I said I would.
“So I am trying here.”
I pressed RE-STEP again.
Still nothing.
“And I don’t know whether you can actually see what I’m talking about from inside that thing. Have you even seen this craft that you’re supposedly flying from?
“Because yes, there’s an airplane here, but it’s inside of a hamster wheel, and there’s a post running straight THROUGH the whole thing. And you, Co-Bobbining, are sitting right there in the middle of it.
“Smack dab in the middle of it.
“Wait. Hang on.”
I left the Computering Room and went down the hall to the bathroom.
I kept a little hand mirror in there.
I brought it back with me, sat back down, and held it out in front of the pretend airplane.
“Here. Take a look at this.
“Can you see yourself in here?
“This is where you’re sitting, because this is what I’m talking about. There’s your airplane. There’s the hamster wheel. And there’s the post going right THROUGH it.
“And look at this. You are also facing the same direction I am when I’m flying this contraption thing, which is strange. If you’re sitting there FOR me, why aren’t you facing TOWARD me?”
I moved the mirror around a little.
“Can you see your plane now?
“Can you see yourself in the mirror?
“Anyway, the point is, I’m out here with a RE-STEP pedal that doesn’t RE-STEP, and I’m trying to get to this Wharf thing, and I’m just trying to give you a sense of what it’s like to be sitting right here right now.
“So let me paint you a little picture.
“Because I’m not sure how familiar you are with the environment around here. You know, how things actually are here for those of us on the Ground where I’m calling from.
“But I was just looking down here TOWARD this area on the floor, you know, where the RE-STEP pedal isn’t working, and then I do notice something here that I do want to pass along as potentially useful feedback.
“And it’s this spot right here.
“So the RE-STEP pedal does not work. We’ve been over this. It doesn’t work FOR me, at least.
“But right next to the RE-STEP pedal is this other little place that is just FOR resting your other foot. It has the little—it’s painted with the little sign, you know, the one that says This One Foot Restinging Thing.
“And that feels strange in a way, you know, because this machine somehow has made it its business to put a little place in the contraption area that’s just FOR this kind of thing.
“You know, a place FOR you to put the foot that isn’t doing anything right now.”
I slipped my foot into it.
I paused and took a deep breath.
“See? After a long day of Assemblementing, it’s actually kind of nice. You’ve been running around putting together a Doll that doesn’t really seem like a Doll, and you’ve been sticking things into other things one part at a time, and you’ve been trying to figure out what everything is FOR.
“And then you come here to take a seat.
“And there it is on the floor.
“This tiny sandal where you can just slide your free foot in FOR a minute.
“But at the same time, I want to be clear: I do stay busy. I’m not the kind of person who sits around all day with my foot in This One Foot Restinging Thing.
“But it seems nice if you could have some kind of balance. You know, one foot gets to rest. The other foot gets to RE-STEP.
“Yeah, and what I’m saying is it would be a very nice balance if this were the kind of machine that actually had an operational RE-STEP.”
I tapped it.
Nothing.
“But you can pass on my feedback about the foot rest to whoever’s in charge.
“And, you know, if it’s helpful, I can give you my name, you know, if you need something like that FOR filing this report.”
“What may I be calling you?”
“Richard.”
I settled my foot a little farther into the Foot Restinging Thing.
“Thank you.
“Because I will say it, it did take me a while to get used to it. But now I think I understand.
“It’s almost like some kind of little invitation from the machine. It’s like they’re saying, Hey, you look like somebody who’s been Assemblementing parts all day, and maybe you’re tired, and maybe you’re not entirely sure how anything fits together anymore.
“So here.
“We took the time to paint this little patch on the floor, and you can rest your free foot here FOR a minute, or however long you want, I guess.
“And your foot can just stay here WITHIN this tiny sandal.
“And don’t get me wrong. I mean, at the end of the day, we do all know we’re in a computer here, right?”
I looked at my foot, resting comfortably.
“Co-Bobbining?
“You know, I may have been a little too harsh with the one star.
“I think maybe we got started off on the wrong foot.
“So I’m willing to think about something that’s more like approaching two. I don’t know whether you’re allowed to change the review after you’ve already called in, but definitely something approaching two, or something in that range.
“Because, you know, I promised I would point out the things that were working FOR me. Not just the things that, you know, seem like they might be broken.
“Or put together wrong, at least.
“And, Co-Bobbining, I said it and I’ll stand by it. I am—”
I stopped.
What was I talking about?
I looked down.
“Oh, right.
“The RE-STEP pedal doesn’t RE-STEP.”
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE, Richard?”
“Haven’t I been telling you this whole time?
“The Wharf.”
I stopped.
“Oh.
“Right.
“That’s what you’ve been asking.
“Yes. This Encounteringmenting Wharf.
“If you are the Co-Captain of this ship thing, you should be helping me figure out how Huckleberry and Enzo can go meet their friends there. I mean, that’s the whole reason I got mixed up in any of this in the first place.
“And I thought that’s what RE-STEP was FOR. You know, to take a step. Go someplace.”
“This RE-STEP stands Usingable FROM HERE.”
“No, it doesn’t. Listen, Co-Bobbining. We just went over this.”
I tapped it again.
Nothing.
“What you stand RE-STEPPING FOR has not yet been furnished FROM HERE.”
I looked at the pedal.
Then at the cup.
“Well, that’s what I’m trying to tell you.
“I don’t know what any of this is FOR.
“But there is this thing here.”
The Steering Wheel.
“And I have to tell you now, this thing works. Like I said, I promised you I would point out those kinds of things too.
“And so this appears to be a working Steering Wheel.”
I turned the wheel, and the whole contraption answered with a tremendous metallic movement.
“See? Something is definitely happening around here when I’m turning this wheel.
“I have no idea what, but something is happening out there on the Rail Line.
“So credit where credit is due. I do appear to be steering.
“It’s just that I don’t know what I’m steering, and I don’t know where I’m steering TO.
“But yes, I suppose this one can go in the working column.
“And what else?
“Oh, this thing over here.”
The big Bellows thing.
“This big Bellows thing over here just blows out air. I’ve tried that one lots of times too. So technically, I guess it works, but I don’t even know which column to put it in. I don’t know why anybody would need something like that in a craft like this—whatever this contraption is—but I can report that air does come out.
“And then there’s this horn thing. It says RE-SOUND.”
I tried it.
“Listen, Co-Bobbining. I’m pressing RE-SOUND and nothing is happening.
“You’d think the machine could, at a minimum, provide a honking horn. So somebody should probably look at that too.”
I surveyed the Traversaling Seat again.
“Oh—and getting back to that little airplane in the hamster wheel. The one I showed you in the mirror.
“This whole time, I’ve been meaning to ask you about it.
“I mean, what is this thing even FOR?”
“This One RE-ZIPPERING Airplane stands RE-Tracting THROUGH This RE-Tractioning Culvert.”
I looked at it.
“That doesn’t even make sense.
“And so what does it do when it’s RE-Tracting THROUGH This RE-Tractioning Culvert?”
“This Airplane stands RE-ZIPPERING ALONG THROUGH.”
I looked at the airplane again.
Then I saw the label.
100% JANUSITE RIBBONATING SUBSTRATIONING INFRASTRUCTIONING
“And what’s with this JANUSITE outfit? It’s everywhere around here. I feel like I’m at an event and there’s only one sponsor, and it’s 100% JANUSITE RIBBONATING SUBSTRATIONING INFRASTRUCTIONING.”
I looked at the airplane again.
And at all of the little toys sitting around the Traversaling Seat.
And I thought of something else to tell Co-Bobbining while I had Co-Bobbining on the line.
“You know what would actually be helpful around here?
“If you would just let me put together something like this airplane myself.
“There are parts all over the place around here. I can find them myself.
“And I think, Co-Bobbining, I could make you a better airplane than the one sitting here.
“At a minimum, I would start by not trying to put a Post directly where the airplane has to fly.
“And if I were to make such a thing, I would put a picture on the box.”
I waited.
Nothing.
“Anyway.”
I looked around.
At the Doll OVER ACROSS there, sitting upon the Unicycle.
I felt my own cup around me.
I leaned in my seat and watched the little cup lean along with my Regard.
I tried the Steering Wheel.
I heard the tremendous movement somewhere in the distance.
I looked at the Bellows.
The string ran from the cup in my hand.
And I said:
“Co-Bobbining.
“What is all of this stuff?”
This time I waited.
“Where am I sitting exactly?”
“You have come to stand sitting WITHIN This One Traversaling Craft.”
I looked around again.
“I’m standing sitting.
“And this is a Traversaling Craft.”
“Yes.”
“Well, tell me, then—at what point is this Traversaling supposed to be happening?”
I looked down at RE-STEP.
“Because so far, Huckleberry and Enzo are exactly as far from that Wharf as they were when I started.
“Meanwhile, I’ve run all around this Rail Line and THROUGH these tunnels, and I feel like I’m standing in the same place where I started too.”
I waited.
Then something else occurred to me.
“And here’s the part that really gets me.
“I supposedly got married to my computer. And the story I heard was that this was somehow going to help me get where I was going here.
“But before I came THROUGH, I already knew how to use my computer. I just sat down in front of it. It had a screen. It had a keyboard.
“I knew where I was because I was looking right at it.
“And now—”
I looked around.
“It feels like the parts between me and my computer aren’t even close together anymore, Co-Bobbining.
“That’s what you need to understand.”
And for once, I couldn’t think of anything else to add.
“THROUGH the PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE-, you may come to stand FROM HERE.”
I listened.
“This is your very own place.”
I looked around my very own place.
“FROM HERE, you may stand THROUGHBY your Terrestrial Computer WITHIN This One Stewardly Co-Occupancyingship.”
“A ship?”
I looked around again.
“Oh.
“So that’s why it feels like we’re floating when I’m looking from up here.”
“Floatationing.”
“Right. Floatationing.”
“FROM HERE, This Place stands as your very own Floatationing Tuple Ship.”
“A Floatationing Tuple Ship.”
I looked around.
“But what in the world is a Floatationing Tuple Ship FOR?”


Gobs– this piece needs to be flagged for insertion into opening of the rail line sequence. 
And there was one Noticingment I did actually stop and read.
It said—
THIS ONE SNAIL HOUSE STATIONING STANDS SITTING UPON THIS ONE GREAT CAULDRON OF CONTINUITY PRESSURE.
I had no idea what that meant.
A cauldron?
Of continuity pressure?
Underneath the Snail House?
Okay.
Whatever you say, PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE-.
I was trying to get to the Wharf.


ADDENDUM — KNOWN CANONICAL / MORPHOLOGICAL CORRECTIONS
The following are already-settled corrections discovered during the later Movement work.
Treat these as authoritative corrections where the standing manuscript clearly refers to the same thing. These are not open architectural questions and do not need to be returned as audit findings.
A. FULL COBBING TITLE
The full ceremonial title is:
This Stewardly Co-Relationing, Co-Ordinationing, Co-Operationing, Co-Bobbining Thing
a.k.a.
The Stewardly Co-Bobbing
Preserve the exact Co-Relationing / Co-Ordinationing / Co-Operationing / Co-Bobbining morphology.
Do NOT globally replace every ordinary Cobbing reference with the full title.
Use the full title where the manuscript is deliberately introducing, naming, ceremonially recalling, or giving the full name of the apparatus/standing — especially in the Movement 23 wrap-up.
Existing shorter/contextual COB forms may remain where they are doing different narrative work.
B. RE-STEPPING CONTRAPTION — ENRICHED NAME
Where the manuscript is referring to the complete RE-STEPPING apparatus and is still using an older/shortened name, the enriched canonical name is:
This One RE-STEPPING AND MAY BE RE-STOMPING Contraption
Do not flatten this back to merely “RE-STEPPING Contraption.”
The enriched name matters because RE-STEP is the Human bodily operation, while RE-STOMP becomes available through the Doll / Little Spooling Unicycle only where the consequential standing actually furnishes that possibility.
Do not infer that every RE-STEP is therefore a RE-STOMP.
C. EN-FIXTURING-MENTING
Audit the later Looking-FOR-Findinging passages for places where we accidentally flattened the continuing standing into a one-time EN-FIXTURING-MENT operation.
For Looking-FOR-Findinging, what matters continues standing:
EN-FIXTURING-MENTING
FOR Looking-FOR-Findinging.
This is not merely a one-time act of placing something into a fixture.
For example, Richard's TABLE may continue standing EN-FIXTURING-MENTING FOR Looking-FOR-Findinging while Richard goes on living his life.
Likewise, Sam's SOME BAG OF COINS may stand EN-FIXTURING-MENTING FOR Sam's Looking-FOR-Findinging.
Preserve EN-FIXTURING-MENT where the noun form is genuinely required by the surrounding grammar; do not mechanically replace every occurrence. But where the manuscript describes continuing standing, prefer the ongoing EN-FIXTURING-MENTING form.
D. LOOKING-FOR-FINDINGING
Preserve the exact morphology:
Looking-FOR-Findinging
Do not normalize this to “Looking-FOR-Finding,” “Looking for Finding,” “searching,” “matching,” or similar conventional language where the canonical operation is intended.
E. LOOKING-FOR-FINDINGING HERE / THERE POLARITY
This is now settled and should be corrected where stale later discussion material contradicts it:
HERE carries what the Constitutioning Human stands Looking-FOR-Finding — continuing EN-FIXTURING-MENTING FOR that Looking-FOR-Findinging.
THERE carries what that Constitutioning Human already has and furnishes outward so another differentiated Constitutioning Human may encounter it under Regard.
Therefore:
RICHARD / TABLE
HERE:
TABLE — what Richard stands Looking-FOR-Finding.
THERE:
SOME BAG OF COINS — what Richard already has and furnishes outward.
SAM
HERE:
SOME BAG OF COINS — what Sam stands Looking-FOR-Finding.
THERE:
TABLE — what Sam already has and furnishes outward.
RICHARD / HUCKLEBERRY / ENZO
HERE:
OTHER CONSTITUTIONING HUMANS ALONGSIDE WITH THEIR DOGS BY-WAY-OF TRAVERSALING ALONG THROUGH FORT INTERCOM DOG PARK — what Richard stands Looking-FOR-Finding.
THERE:
RICHARD ALONGSIDE WITH HUCKLEBERRY AND ENZO BY-WAY-OF TRAVERSALING ALONG THROUGH FORT INTERCOM DOG PARK — what Richard already has standing and chooses to furnish outward, together with whatever photographs/descriptions/other standing Richard elects to make encounterable.
Do not reverse this polarity during first-person conversion.
F. SAM'S TABLE
Protect these differentiated standings:
Richard originally furnished:
CENTRAL POST
Sam's actual Table has:
FOUR LEGS
These do not become semantically equivalent.
Sam furnishes measurements through Correspondencing.
Richard compares those measurements with his actual chair on Ground.
Richard performs the Human Fitmenting and determines that the particular four-legged Table works FOR his Situationing.
Similarly:
QUANTITY 100
upon Richard's SOME BAG OF COINS is not automatically Sam's asking price.
G. OBSERVATIONING CAUSAL ORDER
If the standing manuscript still contains the older Movement 20 test in which Richard deliberately uses RE-SOUND to make the Observationing Class Bell ring merely as a test, that is stale.
Correct causal order:
Relevant actual standing / Hailing occurs.
The appointed Slumbering Lanterning Bugs light first.
Only then does This One Observationing Class Bell ring / RE-SOUND.
Do not have Richard fabricate a correspondent or fake consequential standing merely to test the Bell.
H. RELATIONAL FORM
Where stale manuscript material still uses the old privileged form:
THIS ONE THING
or
FOR THIS ONE THING
for the relevant relational architecture, update it where appropriate to:
THIS ONE SITUATIONING
and:
FOR THIS ONE SITUATIONING
The intended standing is a Situationing Pocket.
The architecture gathers rather than being governed by containment.
I. SUBSTRATIONING
Canonical spelling is:
substrationing
Do not normalize it to “substrating.”
J. JANUSITE
Canonical material name:
JANUSITE
J-A-N-U-S-I-T-E.
Do not use or preserve the stale form “Januite.”
K. COMPOSEMENTING
Canonical morphology:
COMPOSEMENTING
COMPOSE + MENTING.
Preserve the E.
Do not normalize or retain COMPOSMENTING.
L. SMALL-PATCH RULE STILL APPLIES
These known corrections do not authorize broad terminology replacement.
Apply them where the referent and correction are clear.
If an occurrence appears to be doing different grammatical or architectural work, leave it standing and flag it rather than forcing the new form.
Known correction → smallest correct patch.
Uncertain architecture → audit finding, not invention.






MOVEMENT 19 — MY CORNERING COMPUTERING SITUATIONING
Richard is still wearing the Visionizing Plump Croissant Glass Pair.
For a moment, he just stands there.
Then something DOWN TOWARD GROUND catches his attention.
He looks.
His actual Terrestrial Computer is still there.
The same computer he arrived with.
The same computer he supposedly got married to.
And beneath it is the little stand it has actually been sitting on this whole time.
Richard looks at it.
Looks at the computer.
Looks at the stand again.
“You know what?”
A beat.
“I believe you deserve better than that.”
He keeps looking.
“I do.”
“I've had you sitting on that stupid little thing for—”
He stops.
“Well, I'm not gonna say how long.”
“But it's been a while.”
He looks around through the Glass Pair.
Then:
“I think you need a Table.”
A little Bellows becomes available.
Richard recognizes it immediately.
“Oh.”
“Okay.”
He gives it a squeeze.
POOF.
A new Pocket stands available.
Richard looks TOWARD it.
Then RE-STEPs.
The newly POOFed foldingable extent comes into staging ALONGSIDE WITH Richard upon:
THE ROAD WE STAND TRAVERSALING ALONG THROUGH
Richard looks into it.
“Well.”
“All right.”
He takes up the Quilling Stitching Needle.
And begins simply:
TABLE
He looks at the word.
“Yeah.”
“Table.”
“I think I'd like a wooden Table.”
He Quills:
WOODEN
“Not particle board.”
“I've done particle board.”
“Particle board and I have already had our relationship.”
He considers.
“No offense to particle board.”
“But this is my computer.”
He looks DOWN TOWARD GROUND again at the actual Terrestrial Computer.
“She's married now.”
“So.”
“Wood.”
Richard begins Pretending.
The Table begins to acquire standing through his Constitutioning Laboringings.
He furnishes his Terrestrial Computer:
UPON TABLE
“There.”
“And obviously I need somewhere to sit.”
His chair comes into relation:
AT TABLE
Richard regards the little arrangement.
Computer UPON Table.
Chair AT Table.
“Okay.”
“That's already better.”
He studies it.
Then his attention wanders past the Table—
toward the actual room around him.
Toward one particular corner.
“You know…”
“I've actually been thinking about moving my whole computer setup over into that corner over there.”
He looks at it.
“I don't know why I haven't.”
“There's a window there.”
He turns slightly.
“And there's another window over there.”
“So you get light from both sides.”
“Which would be nice.”
“Because right now I've basically got my computer sitting in—”
He looks at the current location.
“Whatever this is.”
He looks back toward the corner.
“No, I like that.”
“That would be a good computering corner.”
He returns his Regard toward TABLE.
“Although…”
He looks at the Table he has been fashioning.
“I don't think I want you rectangular.”
A beat.
“You'd stick out.”
“And I'd hit you every time I came around the corner.”
He Quills:
ROUND
The fashioned Table changes accordingly.
Richard brightens.
“Oh.”
“Yeah.”
“Round.”
“That fits much better.”
He Pretends himself sitting AT it.
Moves his legs.
Immediately finds another problem.
“Although if it's round…”
He looks underneath.
“I don't want four legs down there.”
“Because then I'm gonna spend the rest of my life kicking Table legs.”
He thinks.
“One in the middle.”
He Quills:
CENTRAL POST
The Table refashions.
Richard tries his Pretending legs again.
“Yeah.”
“That's much better.”
He regards the Table.
“How big are you?”
He holds his hands apart.
“No.”
“That's not useful.”
He looks DOWN TOWARD GROUND.
Then goes and gets an actual tape measure.
“See?”
“This is why you keep a tape measure around.”
He pulls it open.
Measures approximately what would work in the actual corner.
“Thirty-six.”
He checks again.
“About thirty-six.”
He returns to the Quilling:
ABOUT 36 INCHES ACROSS
He regards the result.
“Wooden.”
“Round.”
“Central post.”
“About thirty-six inches across.”
“Computer UPON.”
“Chair AT.”
He nods.
“That's a nice little Table.”
Then he looks toward the corner again.
Something else occurs to him.
“Oh!”
“My mom made me that macramé plant hanger.”
He looks around as though he can already see it there.
“I've never really had a good place for that.”
“And I've got the Spider Plant now.”
He stops.
Looks back at TABLE.
Then toward the corner.
Then back at TABLE.
“Oh.”
“This isn't really just about the Table anymore.”
He looks into the corner again.
“I need somewhere for the Table to stand.”
“And the plant.”
“And the computer.”
“And me.”
He starts seeing it.

A beat.
Then he Quills:
MY CORNERING COMPUTERING SITUATIONING
Richard reads it.
“Yeah.”
“That.”
A little Bellows becomes available.
Richard takes it.
“Okay.”

POOF.

He RE-STEPs.
The foldingable extent of:
MY CORNERING COMPUTERING SITUATIONING
comes into staging ALONGSIDE WITH Richard upon the Road.

He leans farther.
“Hang on.”

“No.”
“You're right in my face.”
He shifts his head.
“Nope.”

Nothing useful happens.
“Cobbing?”
“Yes.”
“How do I get this farther away from my face?”
A beat.
Cobbing stands Inquiringmenting AFTER Richard's IN-Questioning-Menting.
“FROM HERE, what may furnish purchase FOR this bearing of your Regard?”
Richard stares.
“What?”
He waits.
Nothing more.
“That's your answer?”
A beat.
“Purchase.”

“Not that kind of purchase.”

Reads:

Something acquires purchase.

“There.”

Then the mouse wheel.

The furnished field of Regard begins to bear away from him—
not straight backward,
but OUT ALONG the curvature of the Gallery.
Richard freezes.
“Oh!”
He rolls it again.
The Cornering extent bears farther OUT ALONG the curve.
More becomes Envisionizingmentingable at once.
Richard rolls again.
And the larger apparatus suddenly begins making visual sense.
“Oh.”
“Oh, look at that.”
For the first time Richard can really see the curve.
The Gallery is not a flat row.
Its differentiated extents stand ALONG a curvature that can articulate while remaining anchored FROM the same HERE.
Richard follows it with his eyes.
“So this whole thing goes around.”
He rolls the wheel a little farther.
“It's a Roundabout Gallery.”
He pauses.
“Of course it's a Roundabout Gallery.”
Along that Roundabout Gallery are standings he recognizes.
Pockets.
Extents he has already worked.
Not gone.
Not erased.
Still standing.
Richard's eyes travel along the curve.
“Oh!”
A little laugh.
“There you are.”
He rolls the mouse wheel slightly the other direction.
The furnished Regard draws nearer.
Then away again.
“Oh, that's nice.”
“That's very nice.”
He settles at a useful compositional distance.
“Okay.”
“Now I can actually see what I'm doing.”
He looks along the Roundabout Gallery.
There is TABLE.
Already fashioned.
WOODEN.
ROUND.
CENTRAL POST.
ABOUT 36 INCHES ACROSS.
Terrestrial Computer UPON it.
Chair AT it.
And there is:
MY CORNERING COMPUTERING SITUATIONING
standing elsewhere ALONG the same articulated Roundabout Gallery.
Richard looks from one to the other.
“Okay.”
“I want you…”
He indicates TABLE.
“…over there.”
He indicates Cornering.
Nothing happens.
“Right.”
He looks at the apparatus.
“You never make it that easy.”
He notices another available operation of the Gallery.
The Anchored Turning Indexing.
Richard tries it.
The Roundabout Gallery articulates.
Not like a carousel carrying Pockets past him.
More like a flexible standing curvature changing its articulation while remaining anchored FROM the same HERE.
The TABLE extent and the Cornering extent begin coming into alignment.
Richard watches.
“Oh.”
“Okay.”
“So I can turn the whole arrangement.”
He adjusts.
“Little more.”
The differentiated openings/extents align.
“There.”
Richard looks at TABLE.
Then Cornering.
He furnishes his own relation:
TOWARD
He pauses.
“Table…”
He looks toward Cornering.
“…TOWARD my computering corner.”
Richard performs an ordinary RE-STEP.
No Buttoning stands Pending.
No RE-STOMP follows.
Nothing launches.
Nothing departs his constitutional locality.
Instead, the differentiated extents RE-FOLD into relation OVER ACROSS the standing Richard has furnished.
The operation settles.
Then something new becomes Outreadingmentingable upon the Road.

“Oh.”

THIS HERE APPROACHING LANDINGING

MY CORNERING COMPUTERING SITUATIONING

“That I can work with.”

Computer still UPON TABLE.

Richard begins placing the composition by Regard.

Somewhere along that standing curvature is the Spider Plant Situationing he fashioned earlier.
Richard pushes his Regard farther OUT along the curve.

Then RE-STEPs TOWARD its existing standing.

Richard looks at his Spider Plant.

Richard nods.

“You can keep that.”
Then he remembers his mother's macramé plant holder.

He gestures with his hands, remembering its shape.

The Spider Plant continues sitting WITHIN its little pot.
The little pot and Spider Plant come to stand WITHIN the remembered macramé plant holder.
Richard looks at the enriched assemblmenting.
“Oh.”
“That's nice.”

Then Richard looks OUT ALONG the Roundabout Gallery again.
He can see the Cornering Situationing standing elsewhere along the curve.

no Pending Buttoning.

TOWARD THE CEILING OF MY CORNERING COMPUTERING SITUATIONING
The macramé plant holder takes its place in Richard's composition.

The Spider Plant sits within the little pot.
Richard looks.
“Oh, yeah.”

“Not directly over the computer.”
He thinks.
“Because I know myself.”

“There.”
“Near the window.”
He regards the whole composition.
The round wooden Table.
The Terrestrial Computer UPON it.
The chair AT it.
The windows.
The light.
His Spider Plant hanging in the macramé plant holder his mother made.
Richard notices another dark spot.

Richard returns to the mouse wheel.
The PITTONING still gives his Regard purchase upon the Roundabout Gallery.

His furnished Regard bears farther OUT ALONG the curvature.
More of the Cornering composition becomes Envisionizingmentingable together.

A quiet beat.
“Yeah.”

Then Richard reaches up.

Ordinary Ground.

Gobs, there is a moment when Richard is training Richard Doll sitting aboard the traversing craft for the first time after richard decides he wants to try pretending. During the moment when we get Richard Doll’s little donut helmet at the Caterpillar Wardrobe Shoppe, let’s have Richard point out to Richard Doll that he can go through the tunnel next to the Vizionizing Croissant Glass Pair dispensing stand when he needs to take something to our currently open pocket. That’s the tunnel that I built with the janusite piping from the RE-Stepping / RE-Stomping contraption earlier in the movie,. 



“And this isn't the first time, Cobbing.”
“This has been going on since the Needle Nozzle.”
“First there was the Needle Nozzle.”
“Then there was the Steering Wheel.”
“Then the Cups.”
“Then the Big Bellows Thing.”
“Then this little airplane business.”
“Then I had to put together the Doll.”
“Then the Doll needed a Unicycle.”
“And every time, there's another Parts Pocket.”
“Another Assemblementing Station.”
“Another bunch of pieces.”
“And somehow I'm the one putting all of it together.”
Short. Checksum. We should use the actual station sequence/names from the standing Giraffe when we print it, of course—not trust my quick enumeration above.
And visually, while he's saying this, he can be indicating the finished things around him.
That's the joke.
“First you made me assemble this.”
Richard touches the thing he is currently operating.
“Then this.”
Another functioning part of the craft.
“Then this ridiculous thing.”
Another affordmenting.
He's making the case that the system has inconvenienced him by making him assemble the very environment in which he is now comfortably sitting, one foot Restingly placed, speaking to COB.
And Needle Nozzle is especially important because it pushes the checksum all the way back to the beginning. The complaint becomes:
Look at everything I've had to do since I got here.
Meanwhile the image says:
Look at everything you've furnished since you got here.
That's beautiful.
It also improves the later line:
“What exactly is all of this stuff?”
Because Richard has just enumerated all of it individually and still doesn't perceive the whole.
He knows:
Needle Nozzle.
This thing.
That thing.
Doll.
Unicycle.
Cups.
Steering Wheel.
Bellows.
RE-SOUND.
Airplane.
He does not know:
This Traversaling Craft.
That's the Gestalt reveal.
And then we get the even larger Gestalt:
This One Stewardly Co-Occupancying Ship.
Then:
Floatationing Tuple Ship.
So 16 is actually doing a beautiful scale change:
PARTS → assembled affordmentings → Traversaling Craft → Ship → Tuple Ship → WHAT FOR?
And Richard's complaint is unwittingly performing the proof.
He thinks he's saying:
Look at all this shit you made me assemble.
The picture is saying:
Yes, Richard. Look at it.
And he's sitting in the fucking thing. 😂
So yes: Needle Nozzle onward gets a checksum. Not another sprawling tangent. Maybe 20–40 seconds, depending on the actual station list. Then the individual control complaints become examples from the completed assembly rather than a disconnected equipment tour.
“I mean, this has been going on since I got here.”
“First I had to put together this Needle-Nozzle thing dragging a string behind it.”
“Then I had to stick this Bobbining thing into a clear box and—somehow—impale my Terrestrial Computer.”
“Then I had to go into this Donut-Helmet-shaped room and pick up another Donut-Helmet-shaped thing and put it over my head.”
I indicated the Donut Helmet.
“Like this.”
“And then there's the Doll.”
I pointed.
“Cobbing, this Doll has problems.”
“This Constitutioning Human Doll can't see.”
“It doesn't have arms.”
“As far as I can tell, it doesn't have a nose.”
“It has one foot.”
“And I put together this whole thing.”
“Every time it's one Parts Pocket at a time.”
“Another Assemblementing Station.”
“Another bunch of pieces.”
“And somehow I'm the one who has to put all of it together around here.”
That's probably already near the maximum useful length. And the Doll deserves slightly more oxygen than the others because “Cobbing, this Doll has problems” is a new comic proposition rather than mere enumeration.
Then IKEA can land as the summation rather than becoming another subject:
“It's like IKEA, except there aren't any pictures on the boxes.”
“I mean, can you at least show me the picture on the box first?”
Done.
And then the Dollhouse sign becomes terrifyingly funny, because Richard has just established his evidence:
Needle → I assembled it.
Clear box/Bobbining apparatus → I assembled it.
Donut Helmet → I assembled it.
Doll → I assembled it.
Next sign: WHOLE DOLLHOUSE ASSEMBLEMENTING STATION.
Richard's inference is entirely reasonable:
Oh, for fuck's sake. Now they expect me to build a house. 😂
And the visual checksum is even better because of what you noticed earlier: while complaining about all the ridiculous Assemblementing he's been forced to perform, he is wearing the Donut Helmet he assembled toward, sitting inside the apparatus his labor has furnished, using its telephone affordmenting to complain to its COB about the apparatus.
We don't underline that. The camera already knows.
Then much later, when he says, essentially, Why don't you let me find some parts around here and put something together myself? I could do a better job, we've made a tiny but consequential turn:
“Why do I have to assemble your things?” → “Why won't you let me assemble mine?”
Richard won't notice the transition.
We will.
MOVEMENT 16 — THIS STEWARDLY CO-CAPTAIN CO-BOBBINING
“HAILINGS & SALUTATIONINGINGS!”
“WHOA!”
I nearly dropped the Cup.
“Hello?”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining now stands attending TOWARD This One Constitutioning Human.”
I looked at the Cup.
“You're going to need to repeat that.”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining now stands attending TOWARD This One Constitutioning Human.”
I strained to make myself heard in the old telephone box's mouthpiece through the Donut Helmet.
I was holding the ear Cup against the side of the Donut Helmet.
“Got it,” I said. “You're some kind of Cobbing.”
“Listen, Co-Bobbing, I'm glad to finally reach customer service around here. Finally, I'm talking to someone who can answer questions about this machine. Because I have more than a few questions.”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining may stand Inquiringmenting AFTER your IN-Questioning-Menting.”
“I'm going to take that as a yes.”
I looked OUT toward the Rail Line.
“First of all, I'm not UN-FOLDING another single thing around here again.”
“Not until somebody tells me what I've been UN-FOLDING myself into.”
“Because this has been going on since I got here.”
“First I had to put together this Needle-Nozzle thing dragging a string behind it.”
“Then I had to go stick this Bobbining thing into this clear box and somehow impale my Terrestrial Computer.”
“Then I had to go into this Donut-Helmet-shaped room and pick up another Donut-Helmet-shaped thing and put it over my head.”
I pointed at my head.
“Like this.”
“And then there's the Doll.”
I pointed toward the Doll.
“Cobbing, this Doll has problems.”
“This Constitutioning Human Doll can't see.”
“It doesn't have arms.”
“As far as I can tell, it doesn't have a nose.”
“And it has one foot.”
“One.”
“And I put together this whole thing.”
“Every time, it's one Parts Pocket at a time.”
“Another Assemblementing Station.”
“Another bunch of pieces.”
“And somehow I'm the one who has to put all of it together around here.”
“It's like IKEA, except there aren't any pictures on any of the boxes.”
“And now the next thing up THERE says WHOLE DOLLHOUSE ASSEMBLEMENTING STATION.”
“I mean, if you're going to make me put together an entire dollhouse, can you at least show me the picture on the box first?”
“So my first question for you, COB, is simple.”
“How many pieces?”
“Just tell me: how many pieces are in that Parts Pocket in that Dollhouse Station Thing up THERE?”
“This One Whole Dollhouse Assemblementing stands FROM HERE as one place.”
“One place? Stands FROM HERE?”
“Yes.”
“That's not what I asked.”
“How many pieces is this one place made out of?”
“You know, how many pieces will I need to ‘stand Assemblementing’ at that station?”
I waited.
“Okay.”
I waited some more.
I checked to make sure the string hadn't fallen out of the Cup.
It hadn't.
Cobbing just didn't have anything more to say.
“Anyway.”
“While I've got you, I do have some comments.”
“And I don't know whether you're the person who handles these things, or if maybe you could pass some of this on to your manager, or whoever runs the place.”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining may stand Inquiringmenting AFTER your IN-Questioning-Menting.”
“Okay, again, I'll take that as a yes.”
“Because I want to be fair.”
“I am not the kind of person who just calls customer service to complain.”
“If something is good, I will tell you it's good.”
“If something is bad, I will tell you it's bad.”
“I don't keep score.”
“I mean, I have a list.”
“But I'm not keeping score.”
“And so far, this is pretty much a One-Star review.”
I thought about it.
“Well.”
“There have been some adequate things.”
“And possibly a few things that have been slightly delightful since the Weddinginging.”
“But there are some real wrinkles with the equipment.”
“FOR me, at least.”
“And if there's something you can do about that—or somebody you can talk to—then I think whoever runs this place might like to know what kind of machine we're all in here.”
“And what kind of machine are we all in here?”
“Well, this one.”
I looked around.
“Cobbing, I assure you. I am not the kind of person who just complains to customer service.”
“If this were my machine, I would want somebody to tell me.”
“Helpful feedback.”
“For example.”
I pointed down.
“This RE-STEP pedal doesn't, you know, RE-STEP.”
“I've tried it.”
“Like a bunch of times.”
I pressed RE-STEP.
Nothing happened.
I pressed it again.
“I'm not sure whether you can hear that through the telephone-box-airplane thing.”
“So let me tell you what you would hear if you were here.”
I pressed it again.
“Nothing.”
“See?”
“How can somebody out here get this fixed?”
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE?”
“Can you hear that?”
I pressed it again.
“I'm pushing RE-STEP over and over again.”
“I'm practically stomping on the pedal to get to the Wharf.”
“And it isn't doing a thing.”
“Which is a problem, because the Encounteringmenting Wharf is the whole reason I'm here.”
“Huckleberry and Enzo are waiting on me to figure this out.”
“And I said I would.”
“So I am trying.”
I pressed RE-STEP again.
Nothing.
“But while we're talking about this area, let me paint you a little picture.”
“Because I'm not sure how familiar you are with the environment around here.”
“You know, how things actually are here on the ground, where I'm calling from.”
“Right next to RE-STEP there's this little spot just for resting your other foot.”
“Technically, I guess, Restinging your foot.”
“And it's strange.”
“Because this machine makes it its business to put a little place in the contraption area just for you to put the foot that isn't doing anything.”
I slipped my foot into it.
“See, after a long day of Assemblementing, it's actually kind of nice.”
“You've been running around putting together Dolls and sticking things into other things and trying to figure out what everything is FOR.”
“And there's this tiny sandal where you can just slide your free foot in for a minute.”
“Now, I want to be clear.”
“I stay busy.”
“I'm not the kind of person who sits around all day with my foot in the Foot Restinging Thing.”
“But it's a nice balance.”
“One foot gets to Rest.”
“The other foot gets to RE-STEP.”
“That would be a very nice balance if this were the kind of machine that actually had an operational RE-STEP.”
I pressed it.
Nothing.
“But the Foot Restinging Thing?”
“That part there is actually kind of nice.”
“You can pass that on to whoever's in charge.”
“Thank you.”
“Because it took me a while to get used to it, but now I think I understand.”
“It's almost like some kind of little invitation from the machine.”
“Like: you've been Assemblementing parts all day.”
“You're tired.”
“You're not entirely sure how any of this fits together anymore.”
“So here.”
“Put your free foot in this little patch for a minute.”
“And it's just a little square on the floor.”
“With a tiny sandal.”

“But still there's a little inscriptioning on the floor.”
I looked at my foot.
“You know what?”
“I may have been too harsh with the One Star.”

“But Two and a Half.”
“Because I promised I would point out the things that were working FOR me, at least.”
“And Cobbing, I said it. And I'll stand by it. I am—”
I stopped.

I looked down.
“Oh.”
“Right.”
“The RE-STEP pedal doesn't RE-STEP.”
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE?”
“The Wharf.”

“Oh.”
“Right.”

“And I thought that's what RE-STEP was FOR.”

“Step.”

The string running from the Cup in my hand.
“Cobbing.”
“What is all this?”
This time I waited.
“Where am I sitting?”
“You stand sitting WITHIN This Traversaling Craft.”
I looked around again.
“This is a Traversaling Craft?”
“Yes.”
“At what point does the Traversaling happen?”
I looked down at RE-STEP.
“Because so far, Huckleberry and Enzo are exactly as far from that Wharf as they were when I started.”
I waited.
Then something else occurred to me.
“Okay.”
“But here's what I still don't understand.”
“I supposedly got married to my computer.”
“Or now I'm supposedly wedded to my computer.”
“And before all this, I knew how to use my computer.”
“I sat in front of it.”
“It had a screen.”
“It had a keyboard.”
“I knew where I was.”
“And now…”
I looked around.
“It feels like our parts don't fit together the way they used to.”
For once, I didn't have anything else to add.
“THROUGH the PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE-, you may come to stand FROM HERE.”
I listened.
“This is your very own place.”
I looked around.
“FROM HERE, you may stand THROUGHBY your Terrestrial Computer WITHIN This One Stewardly Co-Occupancying Ship.”
“A ship?”
I looked around again.
“Oh.”
“So that's why it feels like we're floating.”
“FLOATATIONING.”
“Right.”
“FROM HERE, this place stands as your very own Floatationing Tuple Ship.”
“A Floatationing Tuple Ship?”
I looked around.
“What in the world is a Floatationing Tuple Ship FOR?”


1. COB ANSWERS / RICHARD CAN'T QUITE BELIEVE SOMEBODY IS THERE
Keep your new opening essentially intact.
“HAILINGS & SALUTATIONINGINGS!”
“WHOA!”
I nearly dropped the Cup.
“Hello?”
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining now stands attending TOWARD This One Constitutioning Human.”
“You're going to need to repeat that.”
Repeat.
Then all the wonderful physical business: shouting through the telephone-box mouthpiece through the Donut Helmet, ear Cup held against the outside of the helmet.
“Got it. You're some kind of Cobbing.”
“Listen, Co-Bobbing, I'm glad to finally reach customer service around here.”
Purpose: Relief first. Richard finally has somebody listening.

2. WHAT HAVE I BEEN UN-FOLDING MYSELF INTO?
COB gives:
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining may stand Inquiringmenting AFTER your IN-Questioning-Menting.”
Richard takes it as yes.
Then instead of jumping immediately to pieces, let's enrich the reason for the pieces question:
“Okay.”
“First of all.”
“I'm not UN-FOLDING another single thing around here again.”
“Not until somebody tells me what I've been UN-FOLDING myself into.”
“Because that's how this whole thing has worked so far.”
“I UN-FOLD something, and here comes another notice telling me there's something else I can do.”
“And then apparently I'm the assembly technician.”
“It's like some kind of strange IKEA where there aren't any pictures on any of the boxes.”
“I mean, I built the Doll.”
“I built the whole Doll.”
“And now the next thing up THERE says WHOLE DOLLHOUSE ASSEMBLEMENTING STATION.”
“I mean, if you're going to make me put together an entire dollhouse, can you at least show me the picture on the box first?”
“So my first question for you, COB, is simple.”
“How many pieces?”
“Just tell me: how many pieces are in that Parts Pocket in that Dollhouse Station Thing up THERE?”
Then our settled COB answer:
“This One Whole Dollhouse Assemblementing stands FROM HERE as one place.”
“One place? Stands FROM HERE?”
“Yes.”
“That's not what I asked.”
“How many pieces is this one place made out of?”
“You know, how many pieces will I need to ‘stand Assemblementing’ at that station?”
Silence.
Richard checks the string.
Protect.
This concludes the IKEA/Rail complaint. We do not need another Rail rant later.

3. RICHARD OFFICIALLY COMMENCES HIS CUSTOMER-SERVICE REVIEW
Your new material is doing this beautifully.
Richard explains that maybe COB can handle these matters or pass them to a manager.
COB repeats:
“This Stewardly Co-Captain Co-Bobbining may stand Inquiringmenting AFTER your IN-Questioning-Menting.”
Richard:
“Okay, again, I'll take that as a yes.”
Then the One-Star Review.
I like the overqualification:
“So far, I mean.”
“Well, if pressed, I could probably think of a few things that have been adequate and possibly a few more that may even have been slightly delightful.”
And especially:
“Real wrinkles with how the equipment is feeling so far, FOR me at least.”
Then:
“maybe they'd like to know what kind of machine we're all in here.”
COB:
“And what kind of machine are we all in here?”
Richard:
“Well, this one.”
Protect. That's a terrific exchange.
Then Richard's declaration that he is not the sort of person who complains to customer service, immediately preceding his detailed customer-service complaint.

4. RE-STEP → THE GREAT FOOT RESTINGING EXCURSION
Richard demonstrates:
“This RE-STEP pedal doesn't, you know, RE-STEP.”
“I've tried it.”
“Like a bunch of times.”
Press.
Nothing.
He narrates the silence for COB because COB might not be able to hear it through the telephone-airplane-string apparatus.
COB asks the important question:
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE?”
And Richard spectacularly fails to answer it.
Instead:
“Can you hear that?”
“I'm pushing RE-STEP over and over again.”
“I'm practically stomping on the pedal…”
Then:
“But you should see this, too, Cobbing.”
“I'm not sure how familiar you are with the environment around here.”
“You know, how things actually are here on the ground, where I'm calling from.”
And away we go into the Foot Restinging Thing.
Keep the tenderness you've discovered:
“when you come home from a long day of Assemblementing…”
and:
“it's nice to have a tiny sandal to slide your foot into…”
and especially the recognition that it's almost:
“some kind of invitation from the machine.”
Then Richard revises his review upward.
“Did I say One Star?”
“Because now that I've had a chance to think about it some more, I'm thinking maybe Two and a Half.”
Maybe:
“I don't know if you're allowed to change these things after you've already called them in.”
Could be funny; completely disposable.
Then Richard loses himself:
“And Cobbing, I said it. And I'll stand by it. I am—”
I stopped.
“What was I talking about?”
I looked down.
“Oh.”
“Right.”
“But the RE-STEP pedal doesn't RE-STEP.”
And COB, magnificently, is still standing at exactly the same question:
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE?”
Richard:
“The Wharf.”
Pause.
“Oh.”
“Right. That's what you asked me.”
“The Encounteringmenting Wharf.”
“That's the whole reason I'm here.”
“Huckleberry and Enzo want to go there.”
“And I thought that's what the pedal was FOR.”
“You know. Step. Go someplace.”
Now we finally get:
“This RE-STEP stands Usingable FROM HERE.”
“No it doesn't.”
“Bob, we just went over this.”
Then:
“What you stand RE-STEPPING FOR has not yet been furnished FROM HERE.”
Richard looks at pedal. Looks at Cup.
“That's what I'm trying to tell you.”
“I don't know what any of this is FOR.”
That is the end of luxuriating. Accelerate.

5. THE FAIR AND BALANCED EQUIPMENT INSPECTION
Richard remembers his promise to identify working equipment.
Steering Wheel:
“Okay. This one.”
“This thing works.”
“I promised you I would point out the things that work.”
Turn it.
Enormous metallic motion ALONG THROUGH.
“Hear that?”
“Something is definitely happening.”
“So credit where credit is due.”
“I appear to be steering.”
“I don't know what I'm steering.”
“And I don't know where I'm steering it.”
“But I will put this one in the working column.”
Enough. Move.
Bellows:
“Then there's the Big Bellows Thing.”
squeeze
PFFFFT.
“It blows air.”
“So technically, I guess that works too.”
“I don't know why anybody needs that.”
“But air does come out.”
Move.
RE-SOUND:
“This RE-SOUND horn thing, though?”
try it.
Nothing.
“Doesn't even honk.”
“Somebody should probably look at that.”
Done.
That's the compression we need.

6. AIRPLANE — RICHARD HAS FINALLY FOUND AN OBJECTIVELY DEFECTIVE PRODUCT
Now we give him room again.
“But here's the one I really wanted to ask you about.”
Little airplane.
“What is this thing FOR?”
COB:
“This One RE-ZIPPERING Airplane stands RE-Tractioning THROUGH This RE-Tractioning Culvert.”
Richard works his way through RE-Tractioning.
Then:
“And what does it do?”
COB:
“It RE-ZIPPERS ALONG THROUGH.”
Richard examines it.
Airplane.
Round track.
Post.
“No.”
“No, it doesn't.”
“This one I can tell you.”
“It couldn't.”
“There's a post through the flight path.”
Points.
“Look.”
“Right there.”
“There's a post.”
“Through.”
“The flight path.”
“So unless this is some kind of airplane that can fly through a metal post—which, frankly, would raise several additional questions—this thing isn't RE-ZIPPERING anywhere.”
Then he sees:
100% JANUSITE
“Of course.”
“JANUSITE.”
“More JANUSITE.”
“Let me guess.”
“You people are sponsored by JANUSITE.”
And here's where your new line comes in.
“You know what would actually be helpful?”
“Instead of giving me one Parts Pocket at a time with one thing in it that I'm apparently supposed to put together exactly however you people decided it goes—”
“Why don't you just let me put together something like this airplane myself?”
“There are parts all over the place around here.”
“Let me find some.”
“I think I could build a better airplane than this.”
“At minimum, I would probably try not to put a post directly through where the airplane has to fly.”
That is the last big customer-service flourish.
Maybe one tiny callback:
“And I would put a picture on the box.”
Then stop.
That callback might be wonderful—or one joke too many. Test aloud.

7. RICHARD FINALLY RUNS OUT OF COMPLAINT
This transition needs air.
Richard looks around.
Cup.
String.
Airplane.
Doll.
Unicycle.
Two Cups.
Steering Wheel.
Bellows.
Everything.
Maybe the old Dancing with the Stars line survives here, but very briefly:
“I mean, I have a Doll on a Unicycle.”
“We're sitting in two Cups.”
“And yes, I'll admit the Cups could probably try out for Dancing with the Stars.”
“But…”
Pause.
Now the performance changes.
“Seriously, Cobbing.”
“What is all this?”
“Where am I sitting?”
That's the handoff.
Richard finally shuts up.

8. COB NAMES THE CONTRAPTION
COB:
“You stand sitting WITHIN This Traversaling Craft.”
Richard looks around.
“This is a Traversaling Craft?”
“Yes.”
“At what point does the Traversaling happen?”
Potentially one final dry glance at RE-STEP.
But don't restart the routine.
COB can now make the distinction.
We have two formulations currently competing downstream:
“The RE-STEP is not broken.”
and/or the more architecturally interesting:
“This RE-STEP stands Usingable FROM HERE.”
We've already used the latter above, so I don't think COB needs to repeat both.
Perhaps here COB says only:
“What you stand RE-STEPPING FOR has not yet been furnished FROM HERE.”
Richard now hears that differently because he knows he's sitting in a Traversaling Craft.
Maybe:
“Okay.”
“So it's a Traversaling Craft.”
“The Traversaling hasn't happened.”
“But that's because I don't have something furnished FOR the RE-STEPPING.”
Pause.
“Whatever that means.”
Don't let Richard master it yet.

9. THE REAL PROBLEM: WHAT HAPPENED TO ME AND MY COMPUTER?
This should feel different from the complaint list.
“But here's what I still don't understand.”
“I supposedly got married to my computer.”
“Or now I'm supposedly wedded to my computer.”
“And before all of this, I knew how to use my computer.”
“I sat in front of it.”
“It had a screen.”
“It had a keyboard.”
“I knew where I was.”
“And now I'm sitting in a Cup wearing a Donut Helmet talking to Bob through an airplane.”
Maybe that last line is too joke-forward. Test it.
Then existing excellent line:
“It feels like our parts don't fit together the way they used to.”
That is the emotional question.

10. COB FINALLY GETS SOME ROOM
Now Richard has genuinely yielded.
COB:
“THROUGH the PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE-, you may come to stand FROM HERE.”
Pause.
“This is your very own place.”
This line should have space around it after all the noise.
Richard doesn't necessarily joke.
Then:
“FROM HERE, you may stand THROUGHBY your Terrestrial Computer WITHIN This One Stewardly Co-Occupancying Ship.”
Richard:
“A ship?”
Looks around.
And now his ordinary sensory experience catches up:
“Oh.”
“So that's why it feels like we're floating.”
COB:
“FLOATATIONING.”
Richard:
“Right.”
I would absolutely keep that tiny “Right.” It tells us Richard is already learning how these people talk.

11. LAND ON THE QUESTION THAT OPENS 17
COB:
“FROM HERE, this place stands as your very own Floatationing Tuple Ship.”
Richard:
“A Floatationing Tuple Ship?”
Looks around.
And after everything he has just complained about, the question is now completely earned:
“What in the world is a Floatationing Tuple Ship FOR?”
END MOVEMENT 16.

I think this gives us the accelerated route. The important editorial discovery is that we don't have to shorten Richard everywhere. We give him two enormous comic territories—the Foot Restinging excursion and the airplane—and deliberately make the apparatus between them fast. Then after the airplane, the customer-service performance dies away and the actual question emerges.
And I particularly like the possibility that the last thing Customer-Service Richard says before becoming genuinely curious is essentially:
Give me some parts and let me build the damn thing myself.
Because Movement 17 is standing immediately on the other side of that request.

COBBING:
This One Looping Up Through the Road By-Way-of Looping Down Through the Road Stitching-by-Way-of-Quilling Needle Thing.


Hailings & Salutationingings! Yes. Resetting against the actual standing Movement 16 plus Bill's handoff, I think we now have a much clearer map. And your correction changes one thing importantly: the bug-eyed monitor, mouse, Quilling Needle, and Landinging Dispensary are not four independent jokes. They form one failed diagnostic investigation by Richard.
Here is the movement as I now see it.
Beat 1 — Customer service at last
Keep essentially as standing.
“Hailings & Salutationingings!” → telephone cup → Richard identifies Co-Bobbining as customer service → refuses to UN-FOLD anything else until somebody explains what he's been UN-FOLDing himself into.
This establishes the governing fiction: Richard believes he finally has somebody to whom he can report defects.
Beat 2 — The Assemblementing grievance
Also essentially standing.
Needle-nozzle/string → barboning thing → clear box → TC axle/buckle → Donut Helmet → Constitutioning Human Doll → missing eyes/arms/nose → weird foot → Unicycle → RE-STOMP → endless Parts Pockets.
IKEA without pictures.
Then the Dollhouse Assemblementing Station pushes him over the edge.
Cobbing's:
“This One Whole Dollhouse Assemblementing FROM HERE stands as One Place.”
remains wonderfully unhelpful to the question Richard thinks he asked.
Beat 3 — The one-star review opens the defect inventory
This is where we begin enriching.
Richard establishes:
“I don't keep score.
“I mean, I have a list here, but I'm not keeping score.”
Excellent doorway.
Now his review can start incorporating actual apparent computering defects, not just Assemblementing grievances.
But I would not yet dump the entire monitor investigation here. We need the Rail event to motivate that investigation.
Beat 4 — RE-STEP doesn't RE-STEP / Wharf pressure
Existing material remains.
Richard demonstrates the dead RE-STEP.
Cobbing asks:
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE?”
Richard doesn't hear the question yet because he's busy demonstrating the malfunction.
Wharf/Huckleberry/Enzo return.
This is important because it keeps the whole complaint tethered to Richard actually wants to get somewhere.
And that helps build our later WHERE IS TO? grievance.
Beat 5 — Airplane demonstration → bathroom mirror → macaroni discovery
This existing excursion now gets our first major implant.
Richard wants to prove to Co-Bobbining that the little airplane arrangement is ridiculous.
He goes for the bathroom hand mirror.
Ordinary mirror Regard first: trying to show Cobbing itself, its airplane, the hamster wheel, their shared facing direction.
During fiddling, he flips the mirror.
Magnified/fisheye Regard.
Now he gets close enough to inspect the macaroni-ball structure he could already see.
The big standing is already apparent:
ABOUT painted across the glued-together ball of elbow macaroni standing about the central axis.
Airplane circuit gives him AROUND.
Then magnified Regard reveals the tiny internal inscriptions.
TROUGH.
No—
THROUGH.
And:
“They've written THROUGH through the macaroni.
“Somebody must have a really tiny paintbrush around here.”
That is enough. No theory.
And Richard can begin dismissing this as precisely what he thinks is wrong with the whole place: somebody has made an adorable model and painted relational words on it as though that makes a computer work.
Beat 6 — Foot Restinging Thing / hospitality
Then preserve the existing diversion almost intact.
This is important structurally because after all that dismissal, the machine does something Richard cannot quite dismiss:
it has furnished somewhere for his tired other foot.
His whole emotional temperature changes.
One star → approaching two.
Name acquisition:
“What may I be calling you?”
“Richard.”
Protect all of this. It's one of the movement's hearts.
Beat 7 — Richard finally hears TOWARD
Existing recovery.
Cobbing asks again:
“TOWARD what do you stand RE-STEPPING FROM HERE, Richard?”
This time Richard hears it.
The Wharf.
Then:
“This RE-STEP stands Usingable FROM HERE.”
“No, it doesn't.”
“What you stand RE-STEPPING FOR has not yet been furnished FROM HERE.”
Excellent. Keep.
Beat 8 — Controls inventory
Steering Wheel works.
Big Bellows produces air.
RE-SOUND apparently does nothing.
Little airplane receives its formal claim:
“This One RE-ZIPPERING Airplane stands RE-Tracting THROUGH This RE-Tractioning Culvert.”
“This Airplane stands RE-ZIPPERING ALONG THROUGH.”
JANUSITE.
Richard remains skeptical.
And now his earlier macaroni investigation gives his skepticism teeth. He's not merely saying “that sounds weird.” He's already inspected this apparatus and concluded somebody is painting prepositions on toys.
This is where the TO complaint may find its best culmination:
You've got THROUGH.
You've got AROUND.
You've got ABOUT.
You've been sending me TOWARD things since I got here.
But Richard is trying to go TO the Wharf.
Where is TO?
No Cobbing lecture.
Beat 9 — Rail announces the Never-Ending Approaching Landinging Dispensary
This is the causal change I think we needed.
Rather than treating the little dispenser as another random desktop discovery, the Rail Noticingment makes Richard investigate his computering apparatus.
Rail:
YOU NOW STAND BEFORE
THIS HERE NEVER-ENDING APPROACHING LANDINGING DISPENSARY
Richard:
What?
He looks around for whatever grand thing that could possibly mean.
And then discovers that same absurd inscription on the little sticky-note-dispenser-like affordmenting around the Traversaling Seat/computering assemblmenting.
Now he tries to use it.
Pull a slip.
Another stands.
Again.
Another.
Looks underneath.
Impossible.
Okay—perhaps I'm supposed to write something.
But his keyboard situation is already strange / inaccessible under this Regarding, and there is this elaborate feathered Quilling Needle with an inkwell.
So he tries the obvious Pretending operation:
Quill on the Landinging slip.
Nothing.
Now he's got a legitimate customer-service ticket.
Beat 10 — Richard diagnoses the wrong problem
This is where the bug-eyed monitor and mouse belong together.
He's trying to figure out why none of this works.
His familiar flat monitor doesn't present as a familiar flat monitor FROM HERE.
It looks like a bug-eyed windshield.
Meanwhile, the mouse doesn't behave as expected.
He tries to point at the dispenser.
Whole Regarding changes.
Tries to point at something else.
Another Regarding.
Scroll wheel:
“The scroll wheel doesn't even scroll anything. It just makes everything come at me.”
And Richard puts together a completely reasonable but wrong diagnosis:
somebody installed the wrong monitor.
He's used to a flat screen. This thing is bug-eyed. The pointer won't point normally. The scroll wheel changes how everything presents. Maybe all of these problems are connected.
That's much better than four complaints because Richard is troubleshooting.
And the Quilling failure makes his diagnosis stronger:
I can't point at the thing properly, I can't write on the thing, and this alleged monitor looks wrong.
Therefore: deployment problem.
Wonderful.
Beat 11 — “What is all of this stuff?” → better question
Now the existing scene becomes more powerful.
After exhausting his troubleshooting:
“Co-Bobbining.
“What is all of this stuff?”
Nothing useful.
So Richard improves his own IN-Questioning-Menting:
“Where am I sitting exactly?”
And that Cobbing can answer.
“You have come to stand sitting WITHIN This One Traversaling Craft.”
That's the turn.
Beat 12 — Computer grievance gathers everything
Now the existing late passage:
“But before I came THROUGH, I already knew how to use my computer. I just sat down in front of it. It had a screen. It had a keyboard.”
becomes the summary of evidence we've actually watched him collect.
Flat monitor worked.
Keyboard worked.
Mouse pointed.
Richard knew where his computer was.
Now:
bug-eyed windshield;
mouse changes whole Regardings;
scroll wheel makes everything come at him;
feather pen won't write;
Never-Ending Landinging slips;
macaroni with prepositions painted through it;
airplane in hamster wheel;
parts apparently scattered everywhere.
Therefore his existing line lands much harder:
“It feels like the parts between me and my computer aren't even close together anymore, Co-Bobbining.”
Yes.
Beat 13 — The answer we already have
Then leave the landing alone.
THROUGH the PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE-, you may come to stand FROM HERE.
This is your very own place.
THROUGHBY your Terrestrial Computer WITHIN This One Stewardly Co-Occupancyingship.
Ship?
Floatationing.
Your very own Floatationing Tuple Ship.
And Richard asks exactly the question that opens Movement 17:
“But what in the world is a Floatationing Tuple Ship FOR?”
Then M17 can answer:
“This One Situationing Dollhouse Theater stands FROM HERE as your very own place FOR Pretending.”
So I think that's our surgical map.
The most important improvement is that we're not adding a prop inventory anymore. We've found causality:
Rail says Never-Ending Approaching Landinging Dispensary → Richard tries to operate the corresponding affordmenting → Quilling doesn't work → mouse doesn't point where expected → scroll doesn't scroll → bug-eyed windshield becomes Richard's suspected culprit → he files all of this as evidence that somebody assembled his computer wrong.
That's a scene.
And meanwhile the airplane/macaronis provide the other half of his mistaken diagnosis:
These people have confused Pretending with computing.
That gives Movement 17 exactly the misconception it needs to overturn bodily.
And it's bobbinging thing. I don't know where you guys got barbening thing, but we wouldn't say that.
Yes. Correction accepted: Bobbinging Thing.
“Barboning thing” came from the standing M16 text you pasted, but we should treat that occurrence as stale/wrong and correct it in the surgical print.
So that early inventory beat should refer to the Bobbinging Thing, with whatever surrounding morphology the current standing warrants. I won't propagate “barboning/barbening” further.
And this actually matters for our macaroni observation too: Richard can notice that the elbowed curvature of the macaroni-ball PITTONING Thing vaguely reminds him of curvature he has already seen on the Bobbinging Thing, without concluding that they're the same architecture.
Yeah, and that one we can enrich. It should be from here through, along, through, toward there. And that way it echoes what the airplane says. Yeah. And then I can be like, Yeah, Cobbing, I'm a little out of breath because it took me a while to climb up that ramp. But now that I've gotten close enough to the doll, I see that it's... the doll wasn't the one actually standing in the center, but it's very close to the center. Here's what I'm actually seeing from here. Well, first of all, you should know that there's a sign at the very top of the ramp, and it says regarding bearing from here, whatever we come up with. And it will, the Constitutioning Alongside sign will be there, and it will be confusing to me, and it's okay. I'll just read it out. And we'll also just let Richard say, I got to the top of the ramp, because that's how I'm experiencing it.
Yes. “I got to the top of the ramp” stays. We don't need Richard to speak the machine's geometry before he has any reason to. That's exactly how he experiences it.
And I like the enrichment:
FROM HERE THROUGH ALONG THROUGH TOWARD THERE
because now we have a little rhyme developing across the apparatus. The airplane stands RE-ZIPPERING THROUGH, ALONG THROUGH, while this Regarding Bearing gives Richard a FROM HERE THROUGH ALONG THROUGH TOWARD THERE. We don't need him to notice that rhyme yet.
I think the arrival can be extremely ordinary:
“Okay, Cobbing. I'm a little out of breath. That ramp took longer to climb than I thought it was going to.
“But I've made it to the top.”
Then he looks back toward the Doll/center question he'd been reporting during the climb:
“And now that I've gotten close enough to the Doll, I can tell you something else.
“The Doll wasn't actually the thing standing right in the center like I thought it might be.
“But it's very close to the center.
“Here's what I'm actually seeing FROM HERE.”
That's a lovely little piece of field reporting because Richard has revised his own earlier hypothesis based on getting closer. He's becoming a pretty good observer.
And then before he inventories the computering assemblmenting:
“Well, first of all, you should know there's another one of these signs standing here at the very top of the ramp.”
And he just reads the damn thing.
No understanding required.
Something in the eventual form:
THIS ONE REGARDING BEARING FROM HERE:
CONSTITUTIONING ALONGSIDE WITH THE DOLL
BY-WAY-OF FURNISHING THINGS TOWARD THE DOLL
FROM HERE THROUGH ALONG THROUGH TOWARD THERE
Then Richard:
“Okay.
“I don't know what most of that means.”
“But that's what it says.”
And onward.
I especially wouldn't have him interrogate Cobbing about the sign yet. We've already got enough questioning coming. The comedy here is that Richard has started behaving like a reporter who faithfully reads absurdly specific highway signage into his microphone and then continues:
“Anyway, here's what I'm actually looking at.”
And then we reveal the upper assemblmenting through his eyes.
There's also a really useful distinction emerging between the two halves of the climb.
At the beginning, after PUFF, Richard thinks he is sitting approximately at the center of the whole Circus Tent.
As he climbs and the curve develops, he thinks maybe the Doll occupies the center.
At the top he corrects himself again:
No—the Doll is very close to the center, but not precisely the center.
We should absolutely leave that tiny discrepancy standing.
Because there is going to be something structurally consequential near that center—the articulated region of the Traversaling Craft, the area associated with where the Globe presented during the Tuple Ship deck view, etc. Richard doesn't need to solve that now. He merely notices that his eyeballed center isn't quite the Doll.
And then comes the wonderful tonal collision:
“Anyway, Cobbing, don't be alarmed.”
Because after all this exotic geometry, Richard looks around and recognizes:
his computering room.
That's where I'd let the scene become domestic again.
And I think we've now got a nice little arrival sequence to protect:
long curving climb → Richard revises center hypothesis → “I got to the top of the ramp” → Doll is close to but not exactly center → upright Regarding Bearing sign → Richard reads the bewildering full constitutional inscription → “I don't know what most of that means” → turns away from it → “Anyway, don't be alarmed…” → familiar computering-room assemblmenting.
Then chair.
Then something pokes/presses Richard when he sits.
Then adorable cap.
That feels very shootable—and, importantly, Richard still sounds like Richard rather than a guided tour of our ontology.
Yeah, and we can treat these like almost like burgeoning pro tips that Richard is narrating. Because when I start walking up the ramp from the traversing seat, I can point out to the cobbing, I think even before I step on the— from begin stepping over XT toward XT toward XT toward, etc. I can say, Cobbing, there's one thing you're probably— or there's one thing that you may want to notice when you get here and start walking up this, or walking there. I don't know if this is before or after I start to feel like I'm walking up a ramp. Because at first I think I'm just walking from— I'm walking, oh, here's how we can do it. I'm walking along this paint line on the ground, and it says, so I'm reporting it to the cobbing, and it says, From out of the ground here. Movement 17 locked. And yes, I think the bridge into 18 is much shorter now than it looked when we were still solving the ramp.
The thing I especially like about Groundward Regarding / Upside Regarding is that they give Richard an ordinary bodily vocabulary before he understands any machinery. He doesn't need to say, “I'm changing the orientation of Human Regard.” He's sitting in the Traversaling Seat and discovers: oh, I can look this way; and I can look that way.
I think there are actually three useful terms/functions nearby, and we should keep them differentiated:
Regarding is the activity/apparatus generally: Richard is furnished a Regard upon what stands.
Groundward Regarding is Richard seated, regarding back toward the familiar Ground/computering-room standing—the ordinary orientation that feels like “home.”
Upside Regarding is the complementary bodily Regard into the Dollhouse/Traversaling standing—the side where the Doll, Road, Gallery, and constitutional apparatus become available to him.
And I like Upside rather than something geometrically clinical like “upward.” We've already been using Upside in the architecture. It sounds like something Richard could discover physically: “Oh. There's an Upside to this thing.” It also avoids claiming that one side is universally spatially “up.” It's relational to how the apparatus is inhabited.
The key to 18, I think, is that we don't need to manufacture a bridge to the Spider Plant. We already accidentally built it.
Movement 17 ends with Richard exhausted after sprinting. So 18 can open with him returning to the Traversaling Seat. That's what a Human would do. Sit down. Catch breath. Maybe complain that Cobbing made him run around a Circus Tent chasing a preposition.
And now he's seated in exactly the apparatus we've spent all of 17 earning.
From there, I see a very short chain:
Sit → Groundward Regarding → relief/familiarity → Upside Regarding → Doll → plain clear cap → first In-Stringingmenting appears.
That cap is our bridge.
We deliberately put the plain clear cap on the Doll in 17 and didn't cash it out. So when Richard sits back down in 18 and begins experimenting with Regard, the back of that cap can become available in a way it wasn't from the earlier standing Regard.
And that gives us the first In-Stringingmenting surface without Cobbing announcing a new subsystem.
Then Quilling can return.
And once Quilling/Stringing is operational, Spider Plant doesn't have to arrive as a screenplay contrivance. Richard needs an ordinary thing to try it on. The Spider Plant can simply be among the familiar Groundward computering-room furnishings—or become the first conspicuous ordinary Ground thing Richard chooses because it's sitting there and he knows exactly what it is.
That is potentially very funny because after all this extraordinary machinery, his first serious constitutional experiment is basically:
“Okay, Cobbing. Fine. Let's see what this thing can actually do with my Spider Plant.”
And that finally answers the argument that opened Movement 17.
Remember what Richard's objection was:
Just because I Pretend something is in a computer doesn't mean my computer can do anything with what I'm Pretending.
Movement 18 can answer that objection without Cobbing ever arguing with him.
Richard furnishes his Spider Plant. He starts making distinctions about it. He Quills/Stringings them. Something computationally consequential begins standing.

So I think Movement 18's first movement wants to be surprisingly domestic and quiet after 17's sprint: Richard sits down, catches his breath, discovers he can Regard Groundward and Upside, discovers that the cap has something available upon its back FROM this Regard, and starts poking at it.
Then Quilling Stitching Needle.
Then Spider Plant.
Then we're off.

body first → Richard notices → sign confirms → operation follows.

Well, Mobzno, I've decided that groundward regarding and upside regarding should be used. They're paired regardings.
