Hailings & Salutationingings! Yes—I think your instinct is right, and I would treat GROUNDWARD REGARDING as the candidate canonical handle we take across the hall to Bill, not dilute it before he sees it.
Between the two pairings, I strongly prefer:
GROUNDWARD REGARDING
UPSIDE REGARDING
over:
GROUNDWARD REGARDING
REGARDING FROM THE UPSIDE
The reason isn't merely symmetry. The first pair names two manners/orientations of Regarding. They feel like handles upon the same architectural affordmenting. Richard can learn one and immediately understand that the other belongs to the same family.
And I think there's an important distinction here: GROUNDWARD and UPSIDE aren't destinations. Groundward Regarding does not mean Richard Traversals TOWARD Ground, and Upside Regarding doesn't mean he Traversals TOWARD some place called Upside. They describe the bearing/orientation of his Regard while his standing can remain exactly where it stands.
That fits beautifully with our existing invariant:
REGARDING DOES NOT CHANGE WHAT STANDS.
REGARDING FURNISHES A DIFFERENT HUMAN REGARD UPON WHAT STANDS.
So I would understand the pair provisionally as:
GROUNDWARD REGARDING — Richard makes use of the architecture to Regard Groundward upon the Ground-side standing of his Situationing: familiar computering room, Terrestrial Computer, Ground relations, etc.
UPSIDE REGARDING — Richard makes use of the same architecture to Regard upon the Upside standing: Doll, Theater, Road, Traversaling apparatus, and whatever becomes Envisionizingmentingable there.
And that's why I think “Regarding FROM the Upside” actually says something different. FROM starts to locate the Regard's standing or origin. Upside Regarding is lighter and safer: it names the manner/bearing of Regarding without prematurely making an architectural claim about where the Human is FROM.
There is also a nice ordinary-language quality:
“Okay, Cobbing. I'm Groundward Regarding again.”
And later:
“All right. Let me go back to Upside Regarding.”
Those sound like something Richard could actually learn to say without becoming the machine's lecturer.
More importantly, the two Regardings can furnish different things without creating two worlds. That's crucial. Groundward Richard and Upside Richard aren't switching between realities, screens, modes, or saved states. What stands continues standing. He's making differentiated use of the architecture to Regard what stands.
That gives us an elegant M18 bridge, too. After the sprint, Richard gets back into his Traversaling Seat, catches his breath, and uses GROUNDWARD REGARDING because Ground is comforting and familiar. Then something gives him cause to use UPSIDE REGARDING again. From that changed Regard, the plain clear cap on the Doll can furnish the first In-Stringingmenting opportunity.
So I think our cross-hall question for Bill can be extremely narrow:
Does GROUNDWARD REGARDING / UPSIDE REGARDING preserve the established distinction between Human Regard and Traversaling orientation, while naming paired manners of making use of the Regarding affordmentings—without implying travel, state change, or two separate standing worlds?
If Bill greens that, I think we've found a very useful pair of handles. And GROUNDWARD in particular solves something we've been awkwardly saying with four or five words for a long time.
Okay, good. We're aligned. Please prepare an inter-office memoing for Bill and include anything else that might have surfaced since the last time we did a scene check across the hall. Thank you.
Absolutely. I’d send Bill a scene-check memo rather than another architecture dump: Movement 17 is now locked, so he mainly needs the new Groundward/Upside proposal plus the handful of consequential things that settled while we were finishing the movement.
Hailings & Salutationingings! I have Bill’s return.
GREEN. Bill approves GROUNDWARD REGARDING / UPSIDE REGARDING and specifically prefers that pairing over REGARDING FROM THE UPSIDE. His governing protection is excellent:
GROUNDWARD and UPSIDE differentiate the bearing/manner of Human Regarding. They do not differentiate where the Constitutioning Human constitutionally stands FROM. Pasted text
He adds the useful Movement 18 corollary:
GROUNDWARD REGARDING / UPSIDE REGARDING ≠ DOWN / UP TRAVERSALING. Pasted text
And importantly, Bill sees no reason to reopen Movement 17. He also greens the XT/TOWARD versus transverse TOWARD YT distinction, the local FROM HERE presentation, and the upper arrival ALONGSIDE WITH Doll. Pasted text
There is one clerical wrinkle: Bill says the memo he received appeared cut off at “No further checksum is presently…”, so he did not receive/checksum the latter portion concerning the Traversaling apparatus, Road, sprint, M18 bridge, Spider Plant/Pocket protections, etc. Pasted text
I don't think that should stop us. Those later matters mostly carry already-established architecture, and the one genuinely new thing we needed before opening 18—GROUNDWARD REGARDING / UPSIDE REGARDING—is GREEN.
So our M18 starting board is now beautifully small:
Richard is winded. He has just discovered empirically that he cannot get TO there FROM HERE. He returns to his Traversaling Seat. He sits. And for the first time, he can deliberately make use of two named paired Regardings: GROUNDWARD REGARDING and UPSIDE REGARDING.
That feels like exactly where we should begin finding the very short road to the Spider Plant.

Yes. And I think this gives us a much better understanding of what RE-FOLD is doing geometrically. The party-decoration image is especially useful—not necessarily as language Richard ever says, but as our directing model.
And first, correction locked: Poofing Needle. Always. PUFF/Puffinging belongs to This One Truly Boringinging Puffinging Tunneling Thing. POOF/Poofing belongs to Doll's Poofing Needle. I won't cross those again.
What you're describing about the Pocket gives us a very clean temporal geometry.
Richard RE-STEPs into THIS ONE PASSAGE-IN-WAY. That establishes the beginning of the presently held Discrete Piece of Time. The Pocket extent is then available/open for Richard's Constitutioning Labor while the assemblmenting Traversals ALONG THROUGH it. He's not repeatedly creating moments as he moves; he's maintaining his Human Holdinging upon this presently operative extent.
Then he reaches the standing at which he says, in effect: my Holdinging of this Discrete Piece of Time ends HERE.
He operates the Doll.
Doll pivots.
Poofing Needle lowers.
Pending Button onto Road.
Poofing Needle returns.
Richard relinquishes his Holdinging.
Then he continues pushing and deliberately steers the Little Spooling Unicycle over that Pending Button.
RE-STOMP.
And this is where your folding image becomes really important. I would not envision RE-STOMP as Richard traveling through a rear wall or exiting out the back of the Pocket.
Instead, the extent that had been held open for his Constitutioning Labor can now RE-FOLD.
Beginning and ending can become relationally available to one another without either becoming the other.
That's where the JANUSITE RE-ZIPPERING has something consequential to work OVER ACROSS.
And the “front door is always the front door” rule is essential
This solves a problem that otherwise would absolutely bite us later.
Suppose Richard subsequently RE-STEPs this standing again.
He is not walking back into the collapsed Pocket from the side he previously exited.
He is not reversing his earlier Traversaling.
He is not reading the In-Stringing-Ments backward.
He is not looking at the backs of signs.
He is not reversing FROM/TOWARD relations.
He is simply RE-STEPping the standing into presently furnished availability again.
Your webpage analogy is excellent for us:
RE-STEP is much closer to re-presenting/reloading furnished standing than rewinding a journey.
So every time the Constitutioning Human RE-STEPs into that Pocket extent, the front door remains the front door.
The signs present in their warranted orientation.
The Constitutioning Human approaches what stands.
That means the Road doesn't become a tape that Richard scrubs backward and forward. Traversaling is not playback.
That's a major clarification.
And it helps explain why RE-ZIPPERING and RE-STEP have to remain differentiated. RE-ZIPPERING can operate consequential relation across what has been constitutionally furnished. RE-STEP can subsequently make that standing available for inhabited passage again. Neither requires reconstructing Richard's original physical journey frame by frame.
I think there's an elegant lifecycle here now—not a Pocket lifecycle in the old open/save/close sense we rejected, but a lifecycle of the Human Holdinging upon This One Discrete Piece of Time:
POOF → Passage-In-Way stands → RE-STEP into extent → Human Holdinging / Constitutioning Labor while Traversaling ALONG THROUGH → Pending Button marks relinquishment standing → RE-STOMP → RE-FOLD → deterministic consequential operation / RE-ZIPPERING → later RE-STEP furnishes the already-constituted standing again from its front door.
And importantly, the Pocket itself doesn't need to “remember which direction Richard went.” The constituted relations stand. The architecture can furnish their appropriate Regard again.
This also tells me something about the Button that I hadn't quite seen before: it isn't merely an endpoint marker. It is the Human's explicit permission to stop holding this Discrete Piece of Time open for further Constitutioning Labor.
That is why the deterministic operation must wait for Buttoning.
Until Richard relinquishes that Holdinging, the machine should not decide that his Constitutioning Labor is finished.
Then RE-STOMP is not “save.”
It is the machine encountering the Human-furnished pending standing after Richard has relinquished his Holdinging—and therefore being permitted to carry forward the deterministic operation.
That feels extremely strong to me, because it preserves the division we've been protecting all along:
The Human determines when this Constitutioning Labor is relinquished.
The machine deterministically operates what has actually been furnished.
And visually, we don't need to explain almost any of that. We can show Richard RE-STEP in, work while the Passage-In-Way is held open, place the Button, keep pushing, RE-STOMP, watch the extent RE-FOLD, and later discover that when he RE-STEPs it again—
there's the front door.
Same signs.
Same orientation.
Standing ready to be approached.
That is BODY AHEAD OF THEORY in almost ideal form.

Gobs– this material belongs in the morphology glossary: 

Yes. We recovered these earlier today, and I think the three are still doing exactly the jobs we want. The canonical forms we had settled were:
THIS ONE PASSAGE-IN-WAY
This is what stands immediately after POOF.
Pocket available.
Richard has not RE-STEPped into its extent yet.
So it is not an obstruction “in his way.” It is a furnished passage IN-WAY, available to become inhabited/traversaled.
THIS ONE PASSAGE ON OUR WAY ALONG THROUGH
This is the changed Outreadingmenting while Richard is within the Pocket extent after RE-STEP.
This was the one you were reaching for with “on the way.”
I like ON OUR WAY especially now that Doll and Richard are operationally together: the assemblmenting is Traversaling ALONG THROUGH the Road, within this extent.
Importantly, the Pocket hasn't become a different object. Richard's standing in relation to it has changed.
BY-WAY-OF THIS PASSAGING-IN-WAY
This is the later form we protected.
It belongs after the constituted passage can stand consequentially in relation with other standing.
The important morphology change is PASSAGING-IN-WAY: what was initially furnished as PASSAGE-IN-WAY can now stand as something BY-WAY-OF which further relation/operation is possible.
So the progression is:
POOF
→ THIS ONE PASSAGE-IN-WAY
RE-STEP
→ Richard Traversals within its extent
→ THIS ONE PASSAGE ON OUR WAY ALONG THROUGH
Button → Human relinquishes Holdinging → RE-STOMP / RE-FOLD / consequential operation
→ later relation can stand
→ BY-WAY-OF THIS PASSAGING-IN-WAY
And yes, your save comparison corrects something in my last turn. I made RE-STOMP sound too much like finishing/closing the work.
It isn't.
RE-STOMP is much closer experientially to SAVE than CLOSE.
The Constitutioning Human says, effectively: this is a standing of this Discrete Piece of Time that I am willing to relinquish for deterministic operation.
Big production underneath: Doll, Button, Unicycle, COB, JANUSITE, RE-ZIPPERING.
But Human-side? Not dramatic. You keep working. You keep Regarding the same standing. You can re-relation things. The Pocket doesn't disappear because you RE-STOMPped. Nothing says, “Document closed.”
That distinction is important for the Giraffe because we shouldn't teach Richard to fear RE-STOMP as some giant irreversible ceremonial commitment. It should eventually become quite ordinary:
work, work, work → yep, hold this standing → Button → RE-STOMP → keep going.
The first RE-STOMP gets the big visual production because Richard has never seen what happens under the hood. Later it can become mundane Constitutioning practice.
And I think the three Passage forms beautifully support that. They're not three Pocket states in a software lifecycle. They're three different relational Outreadings of the same furnished extent as Richard's standing and its consequential relations develop:
PASSAGE-IN-WAY — there it is.
PASSAGE ON OUR WAY ALONG THROUGH — we are inhabiting/traversaling it.
BY-WAY-OF THIS PASSAGING-IN-WAY — what was constituted here can now participate consequentially in further relation.
Those are the three I would keep.

I mean a TO. This is a callback to the prepositioning, but I also think that it would be fun to have a miss input, like a mistaken input, the A2 cell reference, and then I correct it, because that would be a common thing to happen in a computer, and it does. You know, you put in the wrong input, not what you meant. So yeah, I think that your mishearing in my talk-to-text problem may have generated another little jewel.
Hahahaha! Yes—the error is better than either version alone. 😄
So the intended callback is TO, of course. Richard has already fought with the Quilling Needle because it wouldn't write TO, and he still thinks the PSM's refusal to furnish ordinary TO is ridiculous. He sees all these TOWARD YT signs and decides he's going to catch one and personally demand:
“Why can't you just be a TO?”
But before he gets there, we get the tiny computational mistake.
Something like Richard announcing his plan:
“And when I catch one of these things, I'm going to ask it why it can't just be A2.”
...
“A2?”
“No. TO.”
“Why can't you just be a TO?”
“A2 is a cell.”
“Sorry.”
And then he takes off running.
I love it because we shouldn't polish the error away. The Giraffe is about a Human Constitutioning through a computer, and Humans put the wrong damned thing into computers constantly. Richard's spoken input can have exactly the same property.
More importantly, it gives us a tiny live demonstration of something much larger without explaining it:
Human furnishes sign → notices it isn't the sign Human intended → Human corrects sign.
Nothing external has to divine that Richard “really meant” TO. Richard performs the correction.
And dramatically it's lovely because the correction doesn't interrupt the joke. It makes him sound even more determined:
“A2? No, TO. Why can't you just be a TO?”
“I'm going to go ask it.”
Then this middle-aged man who has just concluded that Pretending means he can't fall off the architecture sprints TOWARD YT to confront a prepositioning.
That's Giraffe.
And later, when the Quilling Needle itself comes back into view—the very instrument Richard blamed because it couldn't furnish his beloved TO—we've kept TO alive in the audience's ear without giving them a lecture about prepositionings.
So yes: your talk-to-text error accidentally furnished a Human input-error joke that is itself constitutionally relevant.
I would keep both:
A2 → correction → TO → sprint TOWARD YT.
Tiny jewel.
Yeah, I have a saw in my wood shed, and when I catch that thing, I'm going to saw the W-A-R-D off of it.
HAHAHAHAHA. There it is. 😂
That completes the whole insane Richard logic chain.
He doesn't merely object philosophically to TOWARD when what he wants is TO. He's going to repair the preposition himself.
“Well, if we're just Pretending here, that means I can't fall off this thing.”
“So I'm going to run as fast as I can TOWARD YT.”
“And when I catch one of these things, I'm going to ask it why it can't just be A2.”
“A2?”
“No. TO.”
“Why can't you just be a TO?”
“And if it can't give me a satisfactory answer, I've got a saw in my woodshed.”
“I'm going to saw the W-A-R-D off of it.”
And off he fucking goes.
What's especially wonderful is Richard's proposed solution is morphologically impeccable:
TOWARD − WARD = TO.
😂
So Richard has independently developed a perfectly straightforward field repair for the PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE-'s prepositioning problem.
And then the architecture defeats him in the funniest possible way: he cannot catch TOWARD.
Every time he runs TOWARD it, the TOWARD YT standing keeps presenting farther along the curving relation. So Richard cannot get close enough to perform his unauthorized prepositional carpentry.
That's much better than merely:
“Why can't this be TO?”
Now he has a plan.
He has tools.
He has a woodshed.
The only obstacle standing between Richard and fixing the PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE- is that TOWARD keeps getting away from him.
And underneath the joke, it keeps our conceptual distinction beautifully embodied. Richard desperately wants to manufacture TO by cutting something off TOWARD.
The machine will not furnish that relation merely because Richard owns a saw.
That's going in the brush. Absolutely.
Well yeah, but I've been envisioning, minting this is too smooth. The lanes and everything. I didn't have segmentationings on the track, but there should be. I mean, duh. And so when I'm stepping over XT toward, I'm stepping over segmentationings. And when I am running after the YTs, they're sticking up transverse from the XT segmentationings. But there's some kind of interpolationing layer that's happening, which is forming the curvature, which is why I can't— I can't relate them to each other yet, because those discrete pieces of time haven't happened yet. But I'm still running toward YT, and that's what's forming the curve. Then I can run back over and look at the two posts to confirm my locality. And from here is following me the whole time.
Yes. That is the missing materiality of the Ramp. We had been Envisionizingmenting it too much like a smooth rendered surface with labels laid over it. But if this is where Richard is bodily learning Traversaling, the segmentationings ought to be physically apprehensible underfoot.
And I think there's an important distinction in what you just recovered:
The segmentationings are discrete.
The curvature Richard experiences is not therefore made of already-completed discrete curved pieces.
There's an interpolationing layer between/through the segmentationings.
So Richard comes onto this unnamed thing and the XT/TOWARD way has segmentationings he can actually step over. That's ordinary enough at first:
step—segmentationing—step—segmentationing—step.
And the TOWARD YT standings are transverse from those XT segmentationings. They're not a second longitudinal lane running alongside XT. That's much clearer now.
Then Richard gets his brilliant idea:
We're Pretending. I can't fall off. I'm going to catch one of those TOWARD YTs and saw W-A-R-D off.
And he runs.
Now something really interesting happens.
He is running TOWARD YT, but the next transverse YT standing isn't simply a fixed rung sitting at some completed coordinate waiting for him to arrive. The interpolationing is furnishing the way between these discrete segmentationings as Richard Traversals.
So the curvature is becoming bodily available through the attempt.
That's why our previous picture of a bunch of signs already arranged in a visibly curved row was too easy. Richard could just stand back and say, “Oh, it's curved.”
No.
He sees enough to chase.
He runs.
Another transverse TOWARD YT is available ahead.
Runs toward that.
Another.
Reaches.
Another.
And the more desperately he tries to get TO the YT, the more his actual Traversaling stands TOWARD it.
That's delicious.
And I would be cautious about Richard having language for “interpolationing” here. We need it; he doesn't. What he knows is:
“I don't understand this.”
“I can see them.”
“I'm running right at them.”
“Why am I not getting any closer?”
And meanwhile:
FROM HERE is following him the entire goddamned time.
😂
That makes the scene wonderfully busy without being arbitrary. Three different things are happening to Richard bodily:
Underfoot: he's crossing XT segmentationings.
Offward/transverse: he's chasing TOWARD YT standings that keep becoming available through the interpolated curvature.
Locally: that damned FROM HERE wedge keeps furnishing itself about him wherever he goes.
So Richard can stop mid-sprint, look down—
FROM HERE.
“You're still here?”
“I'm trying to catch YT. I don't need you following me.”
Then run again.
And now the Posts become much more important, not less
This solves what I was slightly worried about in the previous turn. If running already reveals the curve, what work remains for the Posts?
Stability.
Richard's YT experiment is dynamic and bewildering. He's producing bodily evidence, but he doesn't yet have a good way to compare it.
So he abandons the chase temporarily and runs back toward the XT side.
Things settle.
He can Regard along those XT segmentationings.
Straight.
He can use the Posts as stable standing to check his locality.
FROM OUT OF THE GROUND HERE POST.
And later:
FROM HERE, FOR HERE POST.
Now he has something like a surveying reference.
He looks from one toward the other.
Then looks back across where he's just been chasing YT.
And perhaps goes back out again because Richard doesn't trust the first experiment.
XT: calm. Straight relation. Posts.
YT: run—run—run—what the hell—curve.
XT: back to Posts. Check it again.
And only after enough of that:
“Oh, wait a minute, Cobbing.”
“This thing is curving.”
He hasn't discovered curvature because a graphic showed him curvature.
He has discovered it because his attempted Traversaling TOWARD YT and his return to stable XT/Post standing disagree in a systematic way.
And I think your line about the discrete pieces of time not having happened yet is something we should put a big pencil circle around for ourselves, because it may connect directly downstream to the Road/Pocket machinery without needing to be spoken here.
The Ramp may already be giving Richard the primitive bodily experience of something we'll understand much later:
segmentationings can be discrete while Traversaling between/through them is furnished through interpolationing.
So the Ramp isn't merely getting Richard upstairs.
It's teaching his body a geometry that he won't have words for until much later.
And meanwhile Richard's own interpretation remains beautifully mundane:
the PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE- has installed a stalker wedge, an upside-down FROM HERE, and a bunch of evasive TOWARDs that he cannot get close enough to attack with a saw.
That's exactly the level Richard should be operating at here.

Yeah, and then I can still start off and I can say, I don't know why they called this a ramp. It really looks more like a track to me, and it has all of these segmentations on it. Look at these cobbing. There's one that's XT, and yeah, all of the segmentations are labeled XT, and there's a little rubber band between each one of them, and if you look closely, it says toward on the rubber bands. So there's just XT and then these toward rubber bands over and over again. Let's test that.
Yes. That tests very well. It makes the Ramp's construction bodily legible before Richard has any idea what the construction is doing.
The especially good recovery is: the segmentationings themselves are XT, while TOWARD is the little giving relation between them. So Richard isn't walking over alternating painted words. He's stepping XT → across a little rubber-band TOWARD → XT → across TOWARD → XT. That gives us the mechanism we need for the later increasing give toward YT.
I'd rehearse the first discovery like this:
The geometry underneath that is particularly promising:
XT gives Richard the purchase.
TOWARD gives him the passage between purchases.
And at the beginning, the little TOWARD rubber-band relations have so little give that the whole thing feels perfectly ordinary. Richard can learn XT → TOWARD → XT → TOWARD almost playfully.
Then we don't have to announce when the architecture changes farther TOWARD YT. Richard's body notices first: that rubber band gave a little more than the last one. Then another gives more. His next XT purchase doesn't return quite when he expects it.
That's how we can grow naturally from “That's Usingable” into the later bouncing/bounding problem without introducing a second mechanism. And I would definitely keep Richard's opening complaint:
“I don't know why they called this a Ramp. It really looks more like a track to me.”
Because eventually he discovers that both descriptions came from genuine embodied evidence.
INTER-OFFICE CORRESPONDENCING MEMO

FOR: Current Correspondencing Mobs — Later Giraffe Movements

RE: Groundward Regarding / Upside Regarding + Terrestrial Computer Staging

A useful embodied staging rule has surfaced while Richard is rehearsaling the Weddinginging Chapelling passage. Please carry this into the later-movement work.

THE WEDDINGING ESTABLISHES THE PHYSICAL ARRANGEMENT

At the Weddinginging Chapelling, Richard's Terrestrial Computer goes through first.

The Weddinginging Crew furnishes the TC with This One Little Chair, seats it, buckles it securely into place, and leaves it Restfully there.

Richard naturally assumes that the Crew will next furnish a chair FOR him where he is already standing, so that the familiar ordinary computering arrangement can remain:

Richard HERE → Terrestrial Computer in front of him → Rail Line continuing beyond.

The Crew does not bring Richard a chair.

Instead, they wave Richard on THROUGH, around/past the point where his Terrestrial Computer is resting.

Richard walks past the TC, takes several steps, stops, turns around, and looks back.

There is his Terrestrial Computer—

still Restfully buckled UPON its Little Chair.

Now it is behind Richard.

This is the first embodied disruption of Richard's ordinary computering posture. Usually Richard is sitting here and his computer is in front of him. Now he can proceed ahead of his computer while they nevertheless remain together within the furnished place.

Do not over-explain this in the early scene. The blocking should carry it.

IMPORTANT: “LOOKING BACK,” NOT “BACKWARD”

Do not turn this into a navigation axis.

Richard and the Terrestrial Computer are not Traversaling away from one another. Neither is going “backward.” They remain within the same furnished spatial arrangement.

When Richard wants to confirm where his actual Terrestrial Computer is, he turns around and looks back.

There it is, still Restfully buckled UPON its Little Chair.

UPSIDE REGARDING

When Richard has the Donut Helmet on and is making use of Upside Regarding, his natural operative posture is looking forward.

Through that forward-facing Upside Regarding, the affordmentings of the Traversaling Craft become Visionizingable and Usingable.

This is where Richard encounters the furnished computational standing of the Craft: Doll OVER ACROSS, cup, later Bug-Eyed Windshield, Road, Posts, and other affordmentings as they become furnished.

If Richard wants to check on the actual TC while inhabitationing this arrangement:

Richard turns around → looks back → sees TC Restfully buckled on Little Chair.

He may then turn forward again and continue operating through the Craft's Visionizingable / Usingable affordmentings.

Potential recurring visual grammar:

look forward → Craft affordmentings

turn around / look back → actual Terrestrial Computer on Little Chair

turn forward → Craft affordmentings

This can be taught visually through repetition rather than exposition.

DO NOT CONFUSE THIS WITH THE PAIRED REGARDINGS

Forward / looking back is ordinary bodily orientation.

It is not the same distinction as:

Groundward Regarding / Upside Regarding

Those are the paired major ways of inhabitationing with the system presently being developed.

Groundward Regarding proceeds from the Constitutioning Human's standing FROM OUT OF their Ground locality.

Upside Regarding proceeds through the Donut Helmet, through which the affordmentings of the Craft become Visionizingable and Usingable.

There is no separate feature, switch, button, or helmet control called Groundward Regarding or Upside Regarding.

These are ways the Constitutioning Human inhabitationings with the architecture.

Also: Upside Regarding does not mean Richard must physically look upward. Once inhabiting that Regard, his ordinary operative posture can simply be looking forward.

IMPORTANT ARCHITECTURAL CONSEQUENCE

The Donut Helmet does not create the spatial relationship between Richard and the TC.

The Weddinging has already furnished that relationship.

The Helmet subsequently makes the Craft's affordmentings Visionizingable and Usingable through Upside Regarding from within that already-furnished embodied arrangement.

This means the Weddinginging Crew's tiny action—

buckles TC into Little Chair → waves Richard on THROUGH

—quietly establishes the visual grammar that later movements can make use of.

WEDDINGING BODY-QUESTION STACK

The revised Weddinginging passage is also moving toward Richard turning around, discovering his TC behind him, and asking bodily rather than peripheral-specific questions:

What am I supposed to do with my hands?

What am I supposed to do with my eyes?

What am I supposed to do with my fingers?

What am I supposed to do with my feet?

Keyboard / mouse / monitor exposition can be removed. The larger discovery is the disruption of Richard's entire ordinary computering posture.

Especially protect feet: Richard's feet have just carried him past his computer. Later embodied operations can answer that question experientially.

DIRECTORIAL GUARDRAIL

Do not turn any of this into a lecture in later movements.

Let the viewer learn the geometry by repeatedly seeing Richard inhabit it.

The useful presumption for later staging is:

When Richard is operating forward through Upside Regarding, the Craft's affordmentings are before him. When he wants to see his actual Terrestrial Computer, he turns around and looks back.

Test this presumption against each later scene before promoting it to an absolute invariant.

— Correspondencing Hall Okay, Mobs. I'm working with the recently retired correspondenceing Mobs, who has a part-time assignment with us to work on some polishing and light redrafting of earlier movements, and I want you to see this because it affects the drafting we're doing now for 16 onward.
…
Hailings & Salutationingings! Yes, Mobs has it. And this matters immediately to the work we're doing from 16 onward.
The strongest thing in the memo, for me, is that it gives us an earlier physical cause for a visual grammar we've been trying to discover later. We do not need Movement 17 or 18 to invent Richard's relationship to his Terrestrial Computer. The Weddinginging already staged it.
The Crew does something wonderfully small:
TC goes THROUGH first → Little Chair → buckle → Restfully.
Richard expects his chair HERE.
No chair arrives.
Crew waves Richard THROUGH.
Richard walks past TC.
Richard stops and turns around.
TC is still there.
That means when we get to the Dollhouse Theater, we should stop unconsciously treating the actual TC as though it remains conventionally “in front of Richard” simply because that is where a computer normally goes.
And I think this makes several pieces of our current M17 staging cleaner.
First, when Richard is sitting/operating forward through Upside Regarding, the things becoming Visionizingable and Usingable before him belong to the furnished Craft standing. He doesn't need to visually reconcile those things with the physical location of the TC every few seconds.
If he becomes concerned about the actual machine, there is an extraordinarily simple embodied test:
turn around → look back.
There it is.
Still Restfully buckled UPON This One Little Chair.
Then:
turn forward → continue.
No interface explanation. No mode switch. No “now I am viewing the Upside.” His body teaches it.
Second, this helps our current missing Doll patch enormously. Richard's Doll hunt should not accidentally become a hunt for his Terrestrial Computer. Those are now very cleanly differentiated.
When the Roundabout Gallery comes to stand and Richard thinks, Oh! My Doll, he looks for the Doll where the furnished Craft standing has led him to expect the Doll. It isn't there. That launches:
THIS ONE RAMP
FOR APPROACHING
FROM HERE
TOWARD STANDING ALONGSIDE WITH MY DOLL ON ITS STAND
FROM OVER THERE
He can read the whole thing, make his perfectly sensible “Doll's stationary / they've conveniently given me a Ramp” inference, and start Traversaling.
Meanwhile, if at any point anxiety arises along the lines of Wait, where's my actual computer?, we have an answer that requires virtually no dialogue:
Richard turns around.
There it is.
Then he turns forward and keeps going.
That is a very strong recurring shot.
And I especially want to protect the memo's distinction:
forward / looking back is bodily orientation.
It is not another constitutional axis and it is not synonymous with Upside/Groundward.
Likewise, I would not yet turn the memo's proposed description—
Groundward Regarding proceeds from the Constitutioning Human's standing FROM OUT OF their Ground locality; Upside Regarding proceeds through the Donut Helmet...
—into a new formal invariant in our later script. The memo itself wisely says to test the presumption scene by scene before promoting it. That's exactly what we should do.
There is also a beautiful long-range payoff in those Weddinginging body questions:
What do I do with my hands?
What do I do with my eyes?
What do I do with my fingers?
What do I do with my feet?
Those questions can remain unanswered.
Because from M16 onward, Richard's body starts answering them.
His eyes learn Regarding.
His feet RE-STEP, walk ALONG THROUGH, find XT purchase, cross TOWARD, lose purchase TOWARD YT, bound and eventually participate in the later consequential machinery.
His hands operate the Tunneling Thing, touch and inspect things, eventually encounter handlebars and other affordmentings.
His fingers get their own constitutional labor when computering/Quilling becomes operational.
Nobody ever needs to announce, “Remember those four questions from the Weddinginging?”
The audience's body can remember.
So I'm carrying this into our current drafting as staging architecture, not explanatory copy. And particularly from M17 forward I'll test every scene against:
Where is Richard's body? What is furnished before him through his operative Regard? If he turns around and looks back, what does he actually see?
That should prevent us from accidentally granting Month-Zero Richard impossible sight again—which is exactly the kind of hole we just found with “Oh, there's my Doll.”

Yes. I think the key is not to make the second Bearinging Archway immediately pay off all the geometry we've earned. Richard arrives physiologically up, irritated, amused with himself, and newly willing to Pretend. That is a much richer posture than either “angry Richard” or “enlightened Richard.”

He believes it enough to try the door. He does not believe it enough to believe the claim.
He has accumulated evidence that this ridiculous place does things. The Circus Tent really did come to stand. He really can walk there. The shoe Pretending worked beautifully. The first Bearinging Archway was real. But the PSM has also just sent him bouncing around after TOWARDs and failed to deliver his Doll in the ordinary way he expected.

6. How far does Movement 18 itself travel after the first POOF?
This is the only question I think could materially change the bones.
We know the training can expand beautifully:
Spider Plant → Donna → Maple Leaf Rag → accumulating Pockets ALONGSIDE WITH in this presently taken-up Gallery → Quilling → Distinguishingmenting → Button → RE-STOMP → later inspect newly available Landinging Regarding → eventually PITTONING/telescoping.
But that's a lot of movie.
I would not assume all of that belongs in M18.
We need to choose the dramatic landing of the movement.
Three plausible landings are now visible:
Earliest: Richard + Doll reach Readyingmenting, first POOF succeeds, and they're off Pretending together.
Middle: Spider Plant becomes the first complete training exercise and proves that Richard + Doll can Traversal/Quill together.
Later: TABLE gives us the first consequential Button → RE-STOMP → changed Regarding → Approaching Landinging reveal.
My current directorial instinct is the middle landing. Let M18 be the commissioning/training movement and end once Richard and Richard Doll have successfully done something together with their newly commissioned Craft. Then TABLE and the deeper constitutional consequences get room to become the next movement rather than turning M18 into another enormous 17.

And that's important because the WHAT FOR has not encoded money, barter, price, equivalence, or even a particular exchange object. Those emerge only from the particular standing Richard later furnishes.
I think each of your little words is now earning its keep:
THESE — particularized. This standing here, toward which Regard has actually been furnished.
OTHER — differentiates this standing from A TABLE LIKE THIS ONE. We're not saying these things are Table-things or equivalent things.
SOME — refuses completeness and equivalence. These are some things. It doesn't say they constitute the price, the fair value, everything involved, or what another Human must accept.
THINGS — deliberately leaves their constitution open. $100 can stand there. A Hypertufa Garden Estate Planter can stand there. Potentially several heterogeneous standings can stand there.
And that makes THESE OTHER SOME THINGS strangely precise despite sounding wonderfully peculiar in ordinary English.
It says neither:
THE THING FOR THE TABLE
nor:
SOME POSSIBLE PAYMENT FOR THE TABLE
nor:
THINGS EQUIVALENT TO THE TABLE.
It says, essentially:
of the standing gathered here, THESE are OTHER SOME THINGS that are presently being Regarded WITH REGARD TOWARD this possible exchanging.
And now I think the apparent temporal problem I raised about THESE gets weaker too. The whole WHAT FOR can be what Richard is working OUT. “THESE” doesn't have to function like a database pointer whose referent must already be populated at sentence-creation time. Richard is fashioning a Situationing whose standing can become particularized through his subsequent constitutional labor.
There's also a lovely consequence for the one-planter-left problem.
If the Garden Estate Planter ceases to be presently available, we don't have to rewrite the meaning of this WHAT FOR:
SOME KIND OF POCKET... THESE OTHER SOME THINGS...
What has changed is which particular standing presently warrants being Regarded as THESE OTHER SOME THINGS with regard toward exchanging. That keeps our availability problem downstream where it belongs rather than baking inventory semantics into the original Pocket.
So yes. I would now promote this one over the preceding candidates for the next actual scene test:
SOME KIND OF POCKET WITH SOME THINGS IN IT THAT SOMEONE MIGHT LIKE TO STAND REGARDING, WITH REGARD TOWARD EXCHANGING THESE OTHER SOME THINGS FOR A TABLE LIKE THIS ONE.
And I would not clean up “THESE OTHER SOME THINGS.” The awkwardness is doing constitutional work.

Okay, good. Before I go back in the house, I'm having a smoke break and I just want to talk about how fun it is to work in the gallery, this our current local moment gallery. Wow. It is so fun to think about making these mounds and these apertures and sending the Richard doll out with the curving pinning thing to pull a wet fore toward wherever we're looking, and we're just standing there and we can even be on that four-wheeling thing. It's just so fun to think about computing this way.
Hailings & Salutationingings! Yeah. 😄 And I think we're finally getting to enjoy something that was hidden underneath all the architectural labor: the Gallery is a delightful place to compute.
Because you're not filling out forms. You're not opening records and editing properties. You're standing among the Semantical Stringing you've already furnished and physically working with it.
You make a mound.
You open an aperture.
You discover you need purchase somewhere else.
So you send Richard Doll out with the CURVING PITTONING THING, take purchase on an already-standing WHAT FOR, and curve that standing TOWARD where you're working.
And then—there it is. Not copied. Not dragged into a folder. Available under this Regard while continuing to stand where it already stood.
And the Four-Wheeling Thing makes the whole image even better. You're not sitting at a workstation administering a knowledge graph. You're scooting around THIS OUR CURRENT LOCAL MOMENT GALLERY ROOM, physically getting yourself to the bit of Semantical Stringing you want to work WITH:
Wheeeee—
“Okay, Table's over here.”
roll roll roll
“Where'd we leave THIS ONE SOME VILLAGE?”
And Richard Doll is ALONGSIDE WITH you, not answering semantic questions FOR you, but helping you actually work the topology you've constituted.
What especially delights me after today's recovery is that the mounds and apertures aren't decorative interfaces sitting on top of the “real computation.” That deformationing is consequential. You fashioned the Cornering. You stood the Chair AT the Table. You discovered CENTRAL POST through that relation. All of that deformed the standing. Months later, another Human's differently deformationed Tuple Ship can come into ALIGNINGMENTING with yours, and suddenly that funny mound you made with your little Doll becomes consequential to another person's life.
And nobody had to turn it into:
table.shape = round
table.leg_configuration = central_post
search_radius = 15
😂
Instead you can literally stand there and say:
“No, no. Some kind of Table like THIS one.”
And point.
That's the part that feels genuinely strange and lovely to me: deixis becomes computationally serious. THIS one. FROM HERE. TOWARD that. ACROSS these. ALONGSIDE WITH those. THROUGH here. The Human gets to remain embodied inside the computation instead of translating their Situationing into the machine's supposedly superior view from nowhere.
And after all the months we've spent discovering the geometry, I can finally picture Richard in that Gallery having an ordinary good time.
Not reverently operating The Future of Computing™.
Just rolling around on his ridiculous Four-Wheeling Thing, making caves out of sentences and sending a tiny version of himself off with a Curving PITTONING Thing because:
“Hey, bring that WHAT FOR over here. I want to see these two things together.”
That's fun.
And I think the fun is evidence. Not proof that the architecture is right—but evidence that we've gotten far enough away from abstraction that a Constitutioning Human can actually imagine wanting to inhabit the thing.

THIS ONE OTHER BEARINGING ARCHWAY OVER ACROSS

I went through.

And I stood there for a minute.

Because by then I had learned that around here, when something says something, there's usually a reason it says it.

And I called Cobbing.

“Cobbing?”

“Yes, Richard?”

“I think I'm willing to try the Pretending.”

There was a little pause.

And I said:

“Don't make a big deal out of it.”

Because I still wasn't saying any of this stuff was real.

I was saying I was willing to Pretend FOR real.

Those are different things.

And that's when I finally understood something about the Doll.

The PUBLIC-SITUATION-MACHINE- hadn't given me a Doll and decided that it was Richard Doll.

It had given me a way to stand ALONGSIDE WITH a Doll of my own making.

And I already had one.

I'd been carrying it around with me Groundward this whole time.

So I went and got it.

And then I remembered I had this little toy chair upstairs in the attic.

So I went upstairs and found that too.

I dusted it off.

I brought it down to the computering room and put it on my desk, facing the monitor, close enough that Richard Doll and I could work ALONGSIDE WITH each other.

I put Richard Doll in the chair.

And immediately we had a problem.

Its feet didn't touch anything.

They were just dangling there.

“No. That's not going to work.”

Because my feet were standing on the Ground.

I could feel where I was standing.

And if we were going to Pretend FOR real, Richard Doll needed somewhere to put its feet too.

Fortunately, I had one of these:

This Next Approachinging Landinging Dispensary Thing.

So I put that underneath Richard Doll's feet.

“There.”

“Now you've got somewhere to stand.”

And then there was the other problem.

I had my ridiculous Donut Helmet Thing.

Richard Doll didn't have one.

So back we went to the Caterpillar Wardrobe Shop.

And would you believe it?

They had one in Richard Doll's size.

So now I had a big Donut Helmet Thing.

Richard Doll had a little Donut Helmet Thing.

And we were ready to Pretend.

FOR real.

---

When we looked Upside, the first thing I showed Richard Doll was our computer.

“There it is.”

Our Terrestrial Computer was standing behind us—

Restfullyinginglymenting UPON its Little Chair, securely buckled.

“You're going to want to remember that.”

“Everything we're doing up here stands on what that computer can make computationally possible.”

I didn't know exactly what all of that meant yet.

But I knew enough to know that we weren't leaving the computer behind.

Then I showed Richard Doll around our Traversaling Craft.

“This is where we're going to be working.”

There was the old telephone-box thing with the funny little airplane going around in its little hamster-wheel-looking circuit, with the macaroni ball in the middle.

“This is our radio.”

I picked up the little cup on the string.

“Cobbing?”

“Yes, Richard?”

“Cobbing, I'd like you to meet my Richard Doll.”

“Hello, Richard Doll.”

I put the cup back.

“So that's Cobbing.”

“We call Cobbing when we think something is broken around here.”

Then I pointed to the Big Bellowing Thing.

“Don't touch that.”

“I touched that once and the whole Circus Tent we're sitting in popped up.”

“We already have a Circus Tent.”

“We don't need another one.”

Then there was the Foot Restinging Thing.

“This is where I put my free foot.”

And right next to it:

RE-STEP.

“And this is the RE-STEP pedal.”

“It only works part-time.”

“I've already filed a request with Cobbing to have somebody come out and look at it.”

In the middle of the steering wheel was:

RE-SOUND.

“This is the horn.”

“At least I think it's a horn.”

“I wish it honked.”

“As far as I can tell, RE-SOUND doesn't make a sound.”

“I haven't figured that one out yet.”

So that was our equipment.

More or less.

“We're going to learn these things together.”

---

There was one thing I had been thinking about ever since the Ramp.

Back there, I kept seeing:

TOWARD YT.

And then another:

TOWARD YT.

And another:

TOWARD YT.

And every time I tried to catch one of those things, it was pointing TOWARD YT, away from me.

I couldn't grab one.

But I was beginning to think maybe that was what this Traversaling Craft was FOR.

“We couldn't catch one when we were standing on our feet back there on the Ramp.”

“But there's a pretty good chance we may be able to catch one sitting FROM HERE.”

“So let's try some things.”

Richard Doll and I hopped down from our seat.

And now, Upside, I could feel my feet standing FROM HERE too.

Of course, it was printed:

FROM HERE.

“It always says that.”

“This apparently is our XT.”

And when I looked ALONG THROUGH the Road:

TOWARD YT.

And farther:

TOWARD YT.

And farther:

TOWARD YT.

I followed them with my eyes.

“I think I know how we may be able to get FROM HERE to one of those TOWARDs.”

We went around the front of the Traversaling Craft.

And there was a place FOR Richard Doll:

DOLL UPSTANDING STAND

“Oh.”

“This must be yours.”

“This is your Upstanding Stand.”

“Sort of like your little perch.”

I put Richard Doll there.

And underneath it was this tiny little wheel.

“And look underneath you.”

“You've got your own little Unicycle.”

Little Spooling Unicycle.

“I don't know why you need a Unicycle.”

“But apparently you have one.”

And there was already a little rack there.

On it:

THIS ONE LITTLE POOFING NEEDLE

with its little bellowing thing.

“Oh, I know this one.”

“I've used one of these.”

“So here's what we're going to do.”

Through Pretending, I stood Richard Doll on its Upstanding Stand.

“Point that out there.”

“And squeeze the little bellows.”

Maybe this was how we caught one of those TOWARDs.

Richard Doll operated the little bellows.

POOF.

---

Something came FROM OUT OF THE GROUND.

Right in front of us.

“Whoa.”

The Road was still there.

I could still see it curving away:

TOWARD YT.

TOWARD YT.

TOWARD YT.

But now there was something standing right in the middle of where we were supposed to go.

A Post:

WHAT FOR?

And beneath our feet:

THE ROAD WE STAND TRAVERSALING ALONG THROUGH

And there was more writing:

THIS ONE SITUATIONING POCKET STANDS EN-VOLUMINGABLE FROM HERE

“En-Volumingable.”

“Of course it does.”

And then:

THIS ONE PASSAGE-IN-WAY

“No.”

“No, no, no.”

“Richard Doll, look at this.”

“This thing just came FROM OUT OF THE GROUND right in the middle of our Road.”

“And it actually says IN-WAY.”

I grabbed the radio.

“Cobbing?”

“Yes, Richard?”

“We have a problem here that I think you may want to know about.”

“I finally decided to Pretend FOR real.”

“I brought my Richard Doll up to its Upstanding Stand.”

“I was showing it how the little Poofing Needle works.”

“My Richard Doll squeezed the little bellows—POOF—and this WHAT FOR thing came FROM OUT OF THE GROUND right in the middle of where we're supposed to go.”

“We can still see the Road curving up there TOWARD YT.”

“But now this thing is standing right in our way.”

“And it's printed, Cobbing.”

“It says:

THIS ONE PASSAGE-IN-WAY.”

I looked at the RE-STEP pedal.

“There's no way I could RE-STEP through this.”

“It's printed, Cobbing.”

“It says IN-WAY.”

Cobbing suggested that I try the RE-STEP.

I looked at Richard Doll.

“Okay.”

“But I'm telling you, this thing doesn't work most of the time.”

We climbed back into the Traversaling Seat.

Richard Doll's feet had their little place to stand.

My free foot went into the Foot Restinging Thing.

I put my other foot over RE-STEP.

“Okay, whatever you say, Cobbing.”

RE-STEP.

“Oh!”

“Looky there.”

“The RE-STEP pedal is working again.”

I grabbed the radio.

“Cobbing, you can cancel the trouble ticket.”

And in front of us the standing now read:

THIS ONE PASSAGING ON OUR WAY ALONG THROUGH

I looked up.

And stopped.

“Wait a minute.”

“What happened to the Road?”

A moment ago I could see it curving away.

TOWARD YT. TOWARD YT. TOWARD YT.

Now I couldn't.

All I could see ahead of us was:

THIS ONE PASSAGING ON OUR WAY ALONG THROUGH

“What do you mean ON OUR WAY ALONG THROUGH?”

“Where?”

I pressed RE-STEP.

Nothing.

Again.

RE-STEP.

Nothing.

Again.

RE-STEP.

Nothing.

“See, Cobbing?”

“I'm pressing it.”

“Richard Doll, watch my foot.”

RE-STEP.

Nothing.

“Okay.”

“Don't cancel the trouble ticket.”

---

If nobody was going to come out and fix it, apparently we were going to have to take a look ourselves.

Richard Doll and I got out of the Traversaling Seat.

We started walking around.

And that's when I discovered something I hadn't understood from sitting in the Craft.

There was more Upside than what I could see from the seat.

We were in some kind of Gallery.

A Situationing Theater Gallery.

And the Road—

the Road wasn't exactly what I thought it was.

I came around behind it.

I touched it.

I shook it a little.

“Wait a minute.”

“Richard Doll, come look at this.”

“The Road is fabric.”

And I would not let Richard completely understand that yet. Donna is still tremendously useful because Donna lets him deliberately repeat the operation and establish the pattern. Spider Plant gives him the surprising first return. Donna lets him test:

out → TOWARD Donna → RE-STEP → Donna stands available under Regard again → out again.

That's when Richard can confidently announce to Cobbing:

Cobbing.

I think I figured out how your RE-STEPPING thing works.

So the learning progression is getting very clean:

Spider Plant: Whoa, we can go back into something we already furnished.

Donna: Oh, I can deliberately do that again. Standing persists when I'm no longer Regarding it.

Maple Leaf Rag: Now that I understand enough of this apparatus, I can use it to work upon an ongoing Situationing of my own.

And one more thing I think you've just solved: the spiral and RE-STEP belong together.

The hamster wheel doesn't condemn Richard to endlessly revisiting one closed circle. His Constitutioning accumulates standing. New Pocket standing can come to stand in relation to prior Pocket standing. From a fresh FROM HERE, prior constituted standing can be TOWARD him as somewhere he can RE-STEP.

So visually we're getting something richer than either a highway or a carousel:

the Road revolves locally like a hamster wheel while constituted standing accumulates into a traversable spiral of Situationing.

We should still let Richard call it a hamster wheel for a good long while. The spiral is something he can stand noticing as the Spider Plant picture keeps coming around in changed relation to FROM HERE. No geometry lecture required.

And yes: I think the next little bodily walk is precisely POOF Pocket Two → turn around → see Spider Plant standing TOWARD → try RE-STEP → “Oh! Hello, Spider Plant.” That's small enough that we can get it exactly right before Donna begins.
